# Cele 697 de capabilități Hermes — status cod

[Statusul sprintului](../HERMES_STATUS.md) · [Reguli și livrări](HERMES_SPRINT.md)

ID-urile H001–H697 sunt poziții în inventarul înghețat, validate prin hash. Folosește căutarea paginii pentru nume/ID sau comanda `show` pentru cerința originală completă. «Audit 07.09» înseamnă moștenit, nu verificat din nou. Dovezile legate sunt fișiere inspectate; simpla lor existență nu dovedește întregul flux și nu afirmă că testele au fost rulate în această livrare.

Fish parcurge recursiv subparser-ele și tratează literal metadatele, fără apeluri către hub sau executarea CLI-ului la completare. Teste cu fish real acoperă fiecare cale curentă, extensii imbricate, prefixe parțiale, comenzi terminale și substituții ostile; Bash/Zsh rămân identice cu baza. Contractul H004 privește arborele de comenzi, nu completarea opțiunilor ori valorilor argumentelor. Scripturile salvate se regenerează după upgrade. Workdir integration rechecked previously-current evidence against base 837d6d08: the parser adds options without changing the command tree or completion implementation. Existing CLI/completion regression coverage is retained. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h042"></a>H042 | Start, check and stop the web UI / headless backend | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Hermes's row is start, check \*\*and stop\*\*, and the stop half is entirely absent: no \`stop\` verb, no pidfile, no ready file. The row cites \`hermes dashboard --status/--stop\` plus backend ready sentinels and a ready file (06-cli-f.md, 16 entries); Nerva has \`nerva status\`, which reads the HTTP surface and so cannot report a hub that is starting or wedged before the port opens, and Ctrl-C. Hermes also signals the conflict as a machine-readable \`BACKEND_PORT_IN_USE\`, where this slice signals it as an exit code and an English sentence — enough for a supervisor, not enough for a caller parsing a status stream. Not delivered either: \`--isolated\`, \`--skip-build\`, \`--no-open\`, the SSH session-token file and owner-nonce, or the interactive auth-setup prompt. \*\*One known limit, in the safe direction:\*\* the probe picks a single address family from the host string while uvicorn binds every address \`getaddrinfo\` returns, so a dual-stack name like \`localhost\` is probed on v4 only and can miss a conflict held on the v6 address — it misses silently, and uvicorn then reports it exactly as it does today. The Windows half of the \`SO_REUSEADDR\` choice is read off asyncio's \`reuse_address = os.name == "posix"\` line and has never been executed here; that test skips off POSIX and the docstring says so. [d1](../serve.py) [d2](../tests/test_h2311_operability.py) [d3](../docs/test-manual/01-environment-and-boot.md) |

| <a id="h117"></a>H117 | Message batching and debounce (treat a burst as one turn) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A burst of messages is one turn. agents/core/channels/batching.py holds the rules. Coalescer (agents/core/channels/batching.py:70) holds turn pieces per (conversation, sender) and merges them in arrival order, joined by newlines, into one turn. Each piece extends the batch window of WINDOW_SECONDS 0.35 (Hermes' _busy_text_debounce_seconds), never past HARD_CAP_SECONDS 1.0 from the first piece (Hermes' hard cap; deadline at agents/core/channels/batching.py:97). A batch of MAX_PARTS 32 pieces flushes at once. JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MS and JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MAX_MS set the window and cap for every channel (configured(), agents/core/channels/batching.py:127); a window of 0 turns batching off. Telegram's poll loop (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:530) hands each update to _handle_update (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:577). It queues what the update reads as: a text fragment, or what an attachment was read as (a photo's description, a voice note's transcript, its caption). So a client-split long message, an album (one update per photo) and a photo followed by its question each become one turn (_queue_turn, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:800). While pieces are held, the loop polls getUpdates with timeout 0 instead of the 25 s long poll (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1157), so the batch flushes when its window closes. A button tap or an observed group message first flushes what was said before it, and stop() waits for the cancelled poll task, then hands what is held to the chat lanes, which get LANE_DRAIN_BUDGET (2 s) to finish (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:346); a failed getUpdates also hands over what is held before its 3 s back-off. A slash command is never merged: what the sender said before it goes first, then the command alone, at once (is_command, agents/core/channels/batching.py:52, CommandRegistry.parse; Telegram's _queue_turn and AsyncBatcher.submit), so /stop after a message still engages the e-stop. The voice mark is keyed by (chat, sender), so another group member's older batch cannot take it, and only the running turn takes it: a reply sent in that turn's context while it runs (_RUNNING_TURN, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:91; _after_reply, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1053). A send from outside that context never takes it (a digest or an outbound notice sent from outside it, another chat's turn, a task an earlier turn left running). One exception is stated at _RUNNING_TURN: once a turn has added an APScheduler job, a job the scheduler fires in that turn's context before the turn ends, and that sends to the turn's chat (the daily digest, in the owner chat), can take the mark. A service line (an unreadable-attachment notice, a transcript echo, a pairing reply, a reason acknowledgement) is sent with voice=False: it is never spoken and never takes the mark. A reply the decision inbox claims as a rejection's reason (H487) is not batched. The poll loop decides with the side-effect-free decision_reason_pending (AutonomyCoordinator.would_consume_reason_reply); a claimed reply, or any fresh text reply in a chat a reason prompt is still being posted to, first hands over what that chat held and is then handled in the chat's lane, where a reply the inbox turns down, or one whose hook fails, is handled as an ordinary message (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:611-639). Its received_at stamp comes from decision_reason_clock, read once per getUpdates page as the page comes back: never later than when that page came back, moved back by Telegram's date for the reply but never before the previous page came back, and after a page cut short by an error the next page keeps the earlier bound (_page_received and _reason_arrival, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:682, :690). The inbox judges the reason window by that stamp, not by when the lane reaches the reply; with no clock, or a failing one, the reply is judged when it runs. Discord and Slack, whose messages arrive as events, use AsyncBatcher (agents/core/channels/batching.py:145). It flushes each batch from its own timer and delivers batches for one key one at a time, under a per-key lock, in order, with at most MAX_PENDING (16) batches on their way and the caller held past that. Slack uses 1, so its one dispatcher waits on a running turn as before batching and its 128-slot ingress queue stays the only buffer. A key's lock is dropped once nothing holds or waits on it. Discord keys by channel and author (agents/core/channels/discord.py:88); Slack keys by channel, member and thread, and with batching off still returns the handler's answer (agents/core/channels/slack.py:365). A stopping Discord or Slack channel discards held pieces and cancels every batch on its way (a timer, a batch waiting behind its key's running turn, and that running turn); a piece or command still waiting for room or for its key is dropped when it wakes, as Slack's shutdown already discards its undelivered event queue. The merged text is what the gateway receives, so its prompt-injection scan runs on the whole message. The gateway scans it with quarantine.detect_injection_normalized (agents/core/channels/gateway.py:176): the raw text plus copies with whitespace collapsed and format characters stripped, hits unioned, so a payload split across pieces, lines or by a zero-width space is caught. Per-chat order is kept: a flush hands the merged turn over before anything later from that chat is processed. tests/test_h117_message_batching.py (60 cases) covers the coalescer (defaults, order, empty pieces, window, cap, wait on the earliest batch, full batch, off) and the Telegram loop: one poll, across polls within the window, after the window, the hard cap, senders and chats kept apart, a photo and its question, an album, a button tap, an observed message, stop, a full batch, batching off, the voice mark, the environment knobs, and the getUpdates timeout. It also covers AsyncBatcher (window, cap, one at a time, off with its answer, full batch with its timer cancelled, an empty batch, drain and discard, a failing delivery logged), Discord and Slack bursts, their stop, and a split injection payload caught in the merged turn. The per-message tests of the Telegram, Discord and Slack channels run with batching off, since they test each message on its own. tests/test_h117_reason_replies.py (79 cases) covers the reason replies with batching on: an ordinary reply still batched, a claimed one behind what its chat said before it and behind a tap read before it, a declined or failing hook handling it as a message, the page stamp and its bounds (Telegram's date, the previous page, a page cut short, a hostile date, a failing or missing clock) and service lines never spoken; tests/test_h487_telegram_reasons.py (34) and tests/test_telegram_voice_reply.py (32) cover the inbox's side and the voice mark left to the running turn. The review round added stop through stop(), a failing poll, command barriers (Telegram and AsyncBatcher), the group voice mark, a zero-width split, lock pruning, the discard of waiting and running batches, a command waiting at discard, the pending bound, and Slack's shutdown and backpressure. There are 61 mutants: 59 killed and 2 equivalent (returning or breaking on a batch that is already gone; a delay of exactly zero); the review round's 18 mutants: 17 killed and 1 equivalent (collapsing whitespace before detect_injection_normalized, which already scans a collapsed copy). [d1](../agents/core/channels/batching.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/discord.py) |

| <a id="h130"></a>H130 | Serve the UI under a reverse-proxy sub-path | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Același bundle SPA funcționează la rădăcină sau sub un JARVIS_ROOT_PATH validat, cu reverse proxy care elimină prefixul. Bootstrap-ul serverului, navigarea și istoricul, API/SSE/streaming, chunk-urile lazy, fonturile, manifestul și service worker-ul folosesc prefixul; cache-urile PWA sunt separate per prefix. Paginile /map și /mission-control legate din SPA sunt adaptate. Teste prin aplicația reală și proxy HTTP verifică reload, shortcut-uri, cache offline, auth pe POST și lipsa cererilor la rădăcina greșită; coliziunile /v2 și /api au regresii. Sub prefix, /metrics păstrează eticheta de rută (middleware-ul de prefix întoarce scope\["route"\] către golden-signals, la fiecare mesaj trimis și la eroare), iar căile nerutate rămân \<unmatched\>. Contractul este pentru browser cu proxy prefix-stripping: wrapper-ul nativ cere prefix gol, /v1 și proxy-urile care păstrează prefixul nu sunt incluse. Fixture-ul dezactivează lifespan/provider startup; nu se pretinde deployment sau probă cu provider live. | <a id="h145"></a>H145 | Read the system logs from the UI | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub's own log is readable from the UI. GET /api/admin/logs (agents/core/routers/admin.py:657) is admin-only and read-only; it reads off the event loop and answers a refused filter with 400, its reason and no-store. Behind it, agents/core/log_tail.py reads the file the hub's own setup_logging actually opened, or reports the error it met (FILE_LOG_STATE, agents/core/log.py:19; _writer_state, agents/core/log_tail.py:95), falling back to the configuration for a process that never set logging up ($JARVIS_LOG_FILE, else data_path('logs','jarvis.log') with system.log_to_file: configured_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:86). It lists only that file and its rotations named with ASCII digits, and only regular files by lstat, so a link, a pipe or a directory is never listed (list_files, agents/core/log_tail.py:114; _listed, agents/core/log_tail.py:105); a file is chosen by one of those names, and it is opened without following a link or blocking and checked to be a regular file (_read_window, agents/core/log_tail.py:197). The read is bounded: at most the last 4 MiB, read in one go and closed before parsing, at most 500 records, each at most 8,000 characters (read_path, agents/core/log_tail.py:279). Lines are grouped into records by the log format's header, so a traceback stays with its error: a record over the limit keeps its header and its newest lines behind a "\[… N lines not shown\]" marker, so the error line is always there; lines above the first header read (a record whose header rotated away, a file not in the log format) are records of their own, beyond the window too. Filters: File, a minimum Level (WARNING shows WARNING, ERROR and CRITICAL; a line with no level is not shown under a floor), a Component (a logger and its children) and Lines (1-500), decided on the record as written, so only returned records are redacted. Redaction runs on every returned field, each line whole before anything is cut, so a secret is never split into a visible half: the H495 secret scanner, an assignment rule for names ending like a credential (\`SMTP_PASS=\`, \`?token=\`, \`password:\`), and the known values of the process's secret-named environment variables and encrypted settings, so it masks at least what /api/admin/env masks (_Redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:159; _known_secret_values, agents/core/log_tail.py:137); when the scanner cannot be loaded nothing is shown, with a note (_redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:181; _record, agents/core/log_tail.py:251). Terminal escape sequences, C0 and C1 controls and bidirectional overrides are removed (_clean, agents/core/log_tail.py:190). The answer carries the components seen (redacted), the bytes scanned and whether only part of the file was read, and an honest note: file logging off and how to turn it on, a file the hub could not open at start, no file yet, or a file that may no longer be written (read_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:327). The HUD's Logs panel (frontend/src/panels/logs.tsx:54, registered in the Console's Observe group) has the File / Level / Component / Lines filters, colours records by level (ERROR/CRITICAL red, WARNING amber, DEBUG dim), shows them newest first as text, never markup, auto-refreshes every 5 s while switched on and the page is visible, skipping a tick while a read is in flight and stopping on a refused credential, shows LIVE only while the hub writes its file, shows no old records after a refused filter (and goes back to the current log when a rotation is gone), and names the admin token on a 401 or 403. \`nerva logs -n N\` (cmd_logs, agents/cli/nerva.py:1329) reads the same bounded, redacted tail (N counts records), says when only part was read, and refuses cleanly on an unreadable file or a missing redactor. tests/test_log_tail.py (33 cases) covers the resolution and the off state, rotations and refused names, records and tracebacks, the caps, the level floor and component children, the byte budget, a partial last line, bytes that are not UTF-8, control characters, redaction (current file, rotation, traceback line, filtered fields), the route and the CLI; tests/test_h145b_log_review.py (32) covers the first review: a secret across the cut (four offsets, and in a continuation line), a long traceback keeping its error line, links and a link swapped in after listing, a pipe, secret-named environment values and credential assignments (and ordinary numbers left alone), a redactor that cannot load (route and CLI), C1 and bidi controls, filtering before redaction, the writer's error and its path, setup_logging's record, no file yet, plain lines beyond the window, the 4 MiB budget, level-less lines under a floor, component redaction, the CLI's records, window note and -n 0, rotation names, a directory and the 400's no-store; frontend/src/test/logs-panel.test.tsx (14) covers the registration, the query, the admin read, order and colours, each filter, the 5 s auto-refresh, a tick skipped in flight, a hidden page, stale records after a refusal, LIVE only while writing, the off note, no match and a partial read, 401 and 403, a kept component and text-only rendering. The first review (two majors: a secret split by the cut came back in part, and a long traceback lost its error line; ten minors; nine nits) is answered; the feature's 29 mutants were caught but one equivalent, and this round's 26, all but one equivalent (the regular-file check after open, which lseek or read also enforces), two after their cases were added (a pipe, the partial first line). Test manual: PNB-169, PNB-170; the API sweep lists the route. Adapted from Hermes: one log file and its rotations instead of six named files, since the hub writes one log; the level is a floor rather than an exact match. Known limits: a record forged by a multi-line message that contains a header-shaped line is shown as its own record (a property of the log format). Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h156"></a>H156 | Edit an agent's persona and description from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | An agent's persona can now be edited in the UI and is live on its next turn. That is Hermes' persona editor, over the version history Nerva already kept. Until now the history was a side record: a commit or rollback in the Prompts panel changed a JSON file while the agent kept its old persona. agents/core/soul_edit.py holds the one path an edit takes, apply_soul (:152). (1) Refusals. An agent the hub has not loaded is refused (404; agent_folder, :100, returns an enumerated folder name in the id alphabet, so no request value reaches a path). So is text over MAX_SOUL_BYTES (256 KiB of UTF-8, :40; 413), safe mode (:171; 409, because overlays are not read there, H275) and a persona the H387 guard would drop (:174; 422 with the verdict). guard_verdict (:60) runs the same scan and cap as Agent._load_soul, and GET /api/agents/{id}/soul now reports it through the same function. A line the guard only quarantines is applied, and the verdict names it. (2) Nothing live is lost. The first edit keeps the file on disk as v1 ("on disk before the first edit"). A file changed on disk since the last version, such as a hand edit, is kept as its own version ("on disk, changed outside the editor") before it is replaced (:185). An agent with no persona file has nothing to keep. (3) Written where the model reads it. The text goes to the owner's overlay (overlay_path, :115): souls/\<id\>/SOUL.local.md in the data home, else the repo-local SOUL.local.md, never the shipped SOUL.md. The write is atomic (_write_atomically, :126: temporary file, fsync, replace; a failure leaves no temporary file and records no new version). (4) Versioned, then live. The text is committed (:195; the same text twice is one version). The agent re-reads its persona (_load_soul, :197), and the result says whether the running agent now reads the written file. (5) Audited without the text. A SETTINGS_CHANGE row names the agent, the version and its hash. A plain commit and a history-only rollback are audited the same way (record_version, :224; agents/core/routers/admin.py:817, :872). Surfaces. PUT /api/admin/agents/{id}/soul {content, message} (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:132, admin). POST /api/admin/prompts/{id}/rollback applies the version through the same path for a loaded agent (agents/core/routers/admin.py:858); the shared contract _identity (served from IDENTITY.local.md) and keys that are no loaded agent keep the history-only rollback. Description. A description: field in the SOUL front-matter is the agent's one-line description (description_of, :84, and description_in, :89: one line, at most 400 characters). It is listed on GET /agents and the SOUL read (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:179, :124) and is never part of the prompt. POST /api/admin/agents/{id}/description/draft (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:150; draft_description, :238) asks the strict-local model for one paragraph from the live persona and saves nothing. With no local model it is a 503, never a cloud call (:255). HUD. The Dossier has an Edit persona button that opens the live SOUL in frontend/src/soul-edit.tsx (SoulEditor, :49, mounted at frontend/src/modes.tsx:96). It offers Preview against the live text, Apply, Draft description with Use it (withDescription, :15, writes the front-matter line), and the hub's refusal in words (refusalText, :32). The Dossier also shows the description. The Prompts panel gets apply beside commit (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2905) and says when a rollback is live. Tests: tests/test_soul_edit_route.py (29) covers the apply, the seed and hand-edit versions, deduplication, blocked and quarantined text, the audit row, size, ids, an unloaded folder, admin, the repo overlay, safe mode, a failed write, no store, a reload that misses the file, rollback (live, to the shipped text, refused, missing, history-only keys, audited), a plain commit, the description (listed, bounded, never prompted) and the draft (a proposal, strict-local, empty). frontend/src/test/soul-edit.test.tsx has 6. tests/test_h10_22_prompt_versioning.py now asserts that the rollback is live, in a throwaway data home. 61 mutants: 58 caught, eight of them after survivors got cases. Two survivors of the first-edit-only seed led to keeping hand edits as versions; that code's own five mutants are caught. The fsync is a crash-only property. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h161"></a>H161 | Warn when the box is running out of memory or disk | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The memory/disk warning Hermes shows is here: ranked conditions, only the worst undismissed one on screen, a suspected OOM restart, dismissal per boot, clearing on confirmed recovery, and a watch that does not depend on autonomy. agents/core/resource_pressure.py holds it apart from the autonomy observer, whose thresholds it reuses (85 % elevated and 95 % critical for memory and disk: DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS, :39, pinned to observer.DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS). (1) Ranking. CONDITIONS (:38) orders disk_critical \> memory_critical \> oom_restart_suspected \> disk_elevated \> memory_elevated. PressureMonitor.snapshot (:259) answers every raised condition in that order, with its level, percent, since and whether it is dismissed, plus the worst one not dismissed. Disk covers every watched volume, and the entry names the paths near or over the line. (2) Volumes. take_sample (:81) reads psutil memory and each distinct watched path once (watched_paths, :283: the filesystem root and the data home). A reading that fails is None, never a healthy 0 %, and changes nothing. (3) Hysteresis. Each resource's _Held.update (:109) raises at once. It clears, or steps critical down to elevated, only after RECOVERY_SAMPLES (3, :40) samples in a row under the line by RECOVERY_MARGIN (5 points, :41). A reading just under the line is not counted and restarts the count, so a flapping reading neither clears nor re-raises. (4) Suspected OOM restart. A small state file in the data home (_write_state, :159, atomic through a temporary file, left behind on no failure) holds the boot id (current_boot_id, :69: the kernel's /proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id, else one from psutil's boot time), the memory level, the dismissals and a clean-shutdown mark. start (:172) finds a run on the same boot that ended without the mark while memory was elevated or critical, and raises oom_restart_suspected naming that level. It logs a warning, and says "suspected" because the kernel's log is not read. stop (:191) writes the mark. The web lifespan calls start before the hub serves and stop as the very first teardown step, so a later step that hangs cannot make a clean stop look like a kill (agents/web.py:644, :650). (5) Dismissal. dismiss (:269) takes a condition and this boot's id. It refuses another boot's id, an unknown condition or one not raised. The dismissal persists across a restart on the same boot; a new boot re-arms it, and so does the condition's recovery, because a new episode is a new warning. Unknown names in the file are dropped. (6) Sampling apart from autonomy. SchedulerService.schedule_pressure_monitor (agents/core/scheduler_service.py:419) samples every 60 s on the hub's scheduler, so autonomy off or ESTOP engaged does not stop it. The route samples on demand when the last sample is over a minute old (fresh, :226). Samples from the route's worker thread and the scheduler are folded in one at a time under a lock. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/system/pressure and POST /api/system/pressure/dismiss {condition, boot_id} are both user-guarded (agents/core/routers/pressure.py:30, :36; 409 on a refused dismissal). The HUD's frontend/src/pressure-banner.tsx is mounted under the power chip (frontend/src/app.tsx:527). It polls the route, shows only the worst undismissed condition in one sentence (an alert for critical and a suspected OOM restart, a status for elevated), and dismisses it for this boot. Tests: tests/test_h161_resource_pressure.py (40) and frontend src/test/pressure-banner.test.tsx (6). They cover the ranking, the volumes, the hysteresis (a margin, a near reading, a step-down, an unreadable sample), the dismissal (this boot, stale boot, unknown, re-armed by a new boot and by recovery, persisted), the OOM suspicion and each of its three conditions, the state file (atomic, damaged, unwritable, failed replace, unknown names), the boot id, concurrent samples, the route, the guards, the scheduler job and the lifespan order. 50 mutants: 47 caught after ten survivors got cases. m11 (raise on \> instead of \>=) is equivalent: a same-level reading resets the count on both paths. The since line was simplified rather than kept as an equivalent. m23 (writing in place instead of the atomic replace) is a crash-consistency property that only a crash mid-write shows. [d1](../agents/core/resource_pressure.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/pressure.py) [d3](../agents/core/scheduler_service.py) |

| <a id="h165"></a>H165 | In-app documentation | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch. Hermes has a Documentation page inside its dashboard with an "open in a new tab" affordance. Nerva's written material (the user guide, FLAGS.md on what each posture flag costs, and the privacy and camera-privacy notes) was only in the repository. The Dockerfile copied it into the image, but nothing served it. (1) The route. agents/core/routers/help_docs.py is registered in agents/web.py (agents/web.py:1527) and user-guarded on the router (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:27). - GET /api/help/docs (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:103) lists the allowlist: slug, title, whether the file shipped, and each document's sections. - GET /api/help/docs/{slug} (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:113) returns the Markdown text, capped at MAX_DOC_CHARS with a truncated flag (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:121). - The allowlist is the fixed DOCS table (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:31): user-guide, flags, privacy, camera-privacy. A slug is only ever a key into it, never joined into a path, so an unknown slug, '../' or encoded traversal is 404. - A file must be a regular file under the install root; a symlink is refused (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:85). A file missing from an install reads as "not available", not an error. - Responses are no-store. (2) The page. DocsPanel (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:37) is registered in the console's Start group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:6), in PANEL_COMPONENTS (frontend/src/gap.tsx:5070) and in the desktop app's route allowlist (desktop/src-tauri/hud-routes.json:49). - It lists the documents, disabling one not shipped, and renders the chosen one through the new shared Markdown component (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101). - That component emits React elements only (no innerHTML path): headings with anchors, code fences, pipe tables, lists, quotes and rules, plus bold, italic, inline code and links. A link renders only for http(s) or a same-origin path, in a new tab with rel=noopener noreferrer (safeHref, frontend/src/markdown.tsx:25). Raw HTML in a document stays literal text. There is no regex lookbehind, so older Safari can parse the bundle. (3) Open in a new tab. Every document is a standalone deep link, /v2/console/docs?doc=\<slug\>\[#section\] (docHref, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:15). The panel offers it as "open in a new tab ↗" with rel=noopener noreferrer (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:67), and a #section is scrolled into view (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:49). (4) The flag's cost where it is flipped, per the row rationale. - Each document's sections carry the names their headings document. The hub computes them with heading_anchor (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:44), which one shared fixture pins character for character to the renderer's headingAnchor (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:94). - The Settings panel links every setting FLAGS.md documents to its section with a "cost ↗" beside the toggle (sectionIndex, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:20; frontend/src/gap.tsx:2754, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2846). - The security posture panel links to the flags document (frontend/src/gap.tsx:1033). The route/auth/OpenAPI snapshots, the HUD parity rules, the API sweep chapter and frontend/src/api/schema.gen.ts are regenerated. Tests: - tests/test_help_docs_route.py, 25 cases: - the list and each allowlisted document (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:35, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:49); - seven off-list and traversal URLs, all 404 (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:67); - the guard (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:71); - a missing file, the cap and a symlink (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:80, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:88, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:94); - the shared anchor fixture and the section index (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:109, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:114, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:122). - frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx: structure, snake_case kept as text, hostile HTML inert, safe links only, no raw-HTML sink and no lookbehind in the source, the same anchor fixture, heading ids. - frontend/src/test/docs-panel.test.tsx: registration, rendering, switching and truncation, deep links to a doc and to a section, and the Settings cost link. When the row was built, all 1,346 vitest cases passed, as did tsc, the e2e typecheck and the build. | <a id="h168"></a>H168 | Shared UI primitives the pages lean on (markdown, destructive-action confirmation, schema-driven fields) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD now has one confirmation for every action that needs one, graded the way the approval queue grades risk, and agent text renders as Markdown wherever it is shown. Both halves of Hermes' confirm-and-render behaviour are covered. (1) One confirmation, graded by RiskTier. frontend/src/confirm.tsx declares RISK_TIER (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:19) with the names and values of agents/core/autonomy/policy.py:26 RiskTier, and tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:22 ties the two lists together. confirmStyle (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:23) gives READ_ONLY one click, REVERSIBLE two steps (the button arms, a second button confirms) and EXTERNAL and IRREVERSIBLE_OR_MONEY a phrase typed exactly (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:143); a tier it does not know takes the most. ConfirmAction (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:74) moves focus to the control that replaced the one pressed. Escape cancels, and a cancelled phrase is not remembered (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:95). Enter confirms only on a match. An action that returns a promise keeps the confirmation open, with both buttons held, until it lands; one that answers false keeps it armed to retry (fire, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:97). A page can open and close it itself (armed/onCancel, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:114, taking focus as it opens, :110) or keep a typed one always open (open: no trigger, no cancel). (2) Every hand-rolled site uses it. The kill switch's HALT ALL stays one click on purpose, because an emergency stop must never wait for a second click; disengage is typed (DISENGAGE, frontend/src/gap.tsx:776, :778). Forget-me types FORGET (frontend/src/gap.tsx:3486) and the acquisition purge its phrase (frontend/src/gap.tsx:4383). Trust-ops rotation types the scope in an always-open confirmation that a refusal leaves filled and a rotation clears (frontend/src/panels/trust-ops.tsx:397). The marketplace uninstall types the folder it deletes, because the rmtree has no undo on this box; a changed folder is typed again, and a refusal keeps it armed (frontend/src/panels/marketplace-admin.tsx:313, confirmRemove :207). The webhook delete is two steps, opened by the row and held while that row's switch is pending (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:420, remove :285). The ESTOP pause is two steps (frontend/src/modes3.tsx:209). tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:26 checks that each of these files imports and uses ConfirmAction and has no window.confirm. (3) Markdown wherever agent text is shown. Chat bubbles, for agent and vision replies (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:78, :95), and decision cards (frontend/src/shell.tsx:144) render through Bubble (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:129). Bubble is memoised on the text, so a streamed token re-parses one bubble. It uses the shared React-only Markdown (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101): lists, fences, tables and http(s) or same-origin links render, while raw HTML and any other link stay text. Canvas Markdown artifacts go through the same renderer (MarkdownBody, frontend/src/artifacts.tsx:78) and are bounded to MARKDOWN_LIMIT (4,000) code points when shown as well as when saved. A new breaks option (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:171) keeps a single newline as a line break for chat and artifacts, and documents keep paragraph joining. The owner's own turns are shown exactly as typed. Test-manual gap G22 (collapsed newlines, fences as prose) is closed. Tests: frontend/src/test/confirm.test.tsx has 11 tests: the grading, click, two steps, typed and case, busy and disabled, a promise and a refusal, a rejected promise, the controlled and open modes, a cancelled phrase, and inert labels. frontend/src/test/markdown-adoption.test.tsx has 6: the agent, vision, user, decision-card and artifact rendering, line breaks, and the bound cut on a code point. frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx gains 1 for breaks. The kill-switch, marketplace, trust-ops and webhooks tests each gain cases, and the purge, marketplace and artifact tests follow the new steps. tests/test_confirm_tiers.py has 2. Of 51 mutants, 48 are caught, eight of them after survivors got new cases. The three survivors were equivalent (the return value of a confirmation the page closes itself, a guard ConfirmAction already enforces, and a pause whose success unmounts the card), and that code was removed. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h182"></a>H182 | Keep the machine awake through a turn, and be aware of power/battery state | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva keeps the machine awake while a turn runs, reads and streams its power state, and eases off on battery, as Hermes does. agents/core/power.py holds one shared power assertion while at least one turn runs (KeepAwake, refcounted). It is off by default and turned on with JARVIS_KEEP_AWAKE=1 (keep_awake_enabled). The mechanism is the platform's own (backend_name, backend_argv): caffeinate -i -w \<hub pid\> on macOS, systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep --mode=block around tail --pid=\<hub pid\> on Linux, so both helpers end with the hub even when it is killed, and SetThreadExecutionState on Windows. The helper is started in its own process group (start_new_session) and released by signalling that group while its leader is still unreaped, so the child systemd-inhibit starts ends too; a helper that already exited is reaped by its handle, and one that ignores SIGTERM is killed after one second. A missing binary or mechanism never fails a turn and is reported (status error), as is a helper that exits on its own. Every turn entry holds it: Orchestrator.handle_input, handle_input_stream and the one-shot process take it at the start (hold_for_turn) and drop it in their finally (release_for_turn), so a reply, an error and a cancel all release it; the lifespan releases every hold at shutdown (agents/web.py) and atexit does at exit. It is gated as host.control (authorize_keep_awake), in the llm_control order: the owner's system-control permission, the host-control contract, whose action list now names power.keep_awake (agents/core/autonomy/remediation.py), an Action Kernel GRANT (the kernel must be on; QUEUE, DENY and anything that is not a GRANT refuse), then a durable audit row (no audit log, or a refused row, refuses too). The action carries the fixed trusted origin, since keep-awake is the owner's switch and not the content of the turn that asks first. It is asked once per process; a refusal keeps it off until the next start and is named in the status. The power state comes from psutil.sensors_battery (read_battery: on battery, percent, plugged). A resume from sleep is seen as the wall clock running more than 60 s ahead of the monotonic clock between two reads (PowerMonitor.tick), which stops while Linux and macOS sleep, and is shown for ten minutes. The scheduler reads it every minute (SchedulerService.schedule_power_monitor, skipped under JARVIS_TESTING), so a resume is noticed without anyone asking. GET /api/power answers the power state, the deferral threshold, whether background work is being deferred and the keep-awake status (enabled, active, holders, backend, refused, error); GET /api/power/stream pushes the same payload to a connected client on every change, with a keepalive while it does not change (agents/core/routers/power.py). Both are user-guarded. Battery-aware throttling: below system.battery_defer_percent (a settings row, 50 by default; 0 never, 100 always on battery) on battery, or on battery with an unknown charge, the heavy background jobs skip their run and return deferred_on_battery, which scheduler health records as skipped: memory maintenance, the tech scout, the learning-loop promotions, prompt evolution and the WorldView KG sync (the last three through SchedulerService wrappers the scheduler now registers). An unreadable power state lets the job run. Nothing the owner asks for directly is deferred. The HUD shell shows a PowerChip (frontend/src/power-chip.tsx): on battery with the charge and whether background jobs wait, a wake from sleep, and keep-awake holding, ready or refused with its reason; a plugged-in machine with keep-awake off shows nothing. It polls GET /api/power every minute and follows the stream where the browser can attach to it. The desktop shell turns webview background throttling off for both windows (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs, background_throttling), so a HUD in the background keeps reading a streaming reply instead of being suspended and having its turn cancelled; NERVA_DESKTOP_BACKGROUND_THROTTLING=throttle or suspend asks for it back and an unknown value is named and ignored (desktop/src-tauri/src/throttling.rs). macOS 14+ honours the policy; Linux and Windows webviews ignore it. The binding-writer inventory's pinned lines in scheduler_service.py move with the file. Tests: tests/test_h182_power.py (76), including a real helper process whose own child is ended by the release, and rustc unit tests of throttling.rs; src/test/power-chip.test.tsx (6). 100 mutants: 98 killed after five cases were added; the other two were redundant checks, removed. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. [d1](../agents/core/power.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/power.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h209"></a>H209 | Keyboard shortcut customisation with a recorder and conflict detection | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD's keyboard shortcuts live in one registry, as in Hermes. frontend/src/shortcuts.ts defines every action once (ACTIONS: id, category, label, default chord, scope): the ten mode keys, World (w), the Console (\`), Ambient (a), Cinema (m) and its three stages (o, n, b, scope cinema), focus the message box (/), the command palette (mod+k) and the Keyboard Shortcuts panel (mod+/). app.tsx (World's key included: WorldAwareApp passes onWorld and shares the live overrides through useShortcutOverrides) and the cinema overlay in shell.tsx dispatch through matchAction over bindings(overrides) instead of literal key maps, and every key hint (palette, top bar, World, Console, cinema stages) is read from the registry (chordOf, displayChord). A matched action takes the key from the browser (preventDefault); Shift+letter falls back to the bare letter unless it is bound itself. frontend/src/shortcuts-panel.tsx is the panel mod+/ opens: categories Navigation, View, Composer and Session with search (searchBindings); click a chord to record a new one ("Press a key…", Esc cancels, a lone modifier keeps listening; chordFromEvent, rebind); the palette and the panel take only a chord held with Ctrl/⌘ or Alt (acceptsChord), fire in an input only then (firesInInputs), and the palette always has a Keyboard shortcuts entry, so no rebinding can lock the panel out; Space is spelled "space", and a chord with no key is refused on record and on read; Esc closes the panel without closing what lies beneath; per-binding reset and Reset all (resetBinding); an "Also bound to X" note when two actions share a chord (conflictsFor). Overrides persist per viewer in localStorage under hud.shortcuts, validated on read (readOverrides, loadOverrides, saveOverrides), and survive storage that is off. Governance: defineActions refuses any action whose id, category or label names an approval, a decision, an accept, reject, deny or consent (FORBIDDEN_TARGET), so no chord can reach a Decision Inbox action. Tests: frontend/src/test/shortcuts.test.tsx (25), app-routing.test.tsx (+14: panel, rebinding, persistence, World, composer focus, the Space and Ctrl+Space lockout, the palette entry, the typing guard, preventDefault, World behind the panel, storage off, live hints, Esc beneath, Cinema), cinema.test.tsx (+1: rebound stage keys); voice-orb and briefing-wall expect the registry's stage titles; 38 mutants, 36 killed, 2 equivalent; the review round's 19 mutants, all killed (4 by tests added after a first pass). | <a id="h222"></a>H222 | Always-visible microphone / wake-word state indicator | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva shows whether it is listening outside its main window, as Hermes does. agents/core/voice/listening.py keeps one process-wide, thread-safe listening state per source (ListeningState, STATE). The host's own voice path publishes it: the wake-word detector marks hub armed once it holds the mic open and off when it stops (WakeWordDetector.start, stop; agents/core/voice/wake_word.py), and a capture marks hub listening while it records, thinking while it transcribes and answers, speaking while it plays the reply, and falls back to armed (or off when the detector is not running) in a finally, so an early end or a failure never leaves it lit (VoicePipeline._capture_and_process; stop marks it off; agents/core/voice/pipeline.py). Wyoming satellites publish it too: an authenticated satellite's detection or voice-started marks satellite:\<id\> listening and voice-stopped marks it thinking; a transcript marks it thinking while it is answered and clears it after; a closed connection clears whatever it lit; with authentication required an unauthenticated peer can set nothing, and with authentication off the one anonymous satellite is named satellite (LISTEN_EVENTS, WyomingServer._listening_source, handle_connection; agents/core/voice/wyoming.py). The API is get / set / on-change in process: set_state (a bounded, printable source name and one of off, armed, listening, thinking, speaking; off forgets the source; at most 32 sources), snapshot (each source's state, the loudest by ORDER — listening, then armed, both an open mic, then thinking, speaking, off — mic_open, and a seq that moves on every change), subscribe and unsubscribe (callbacks run outside the lock and a failing one is ignored). A transient state not re-said for 120 s expires, and the expiry moves seq, so a crashed capture never leaves the indicator on; an armed mic stays. GET /api/voice/listening answers the snapshot and GET /api/voice/listening/stream pushes it on every change with keepalives (agents/core/routers/voice.py); both are user-guarded and GET-only, so nothing can set the state over HTTP. Every writer and reader loads the one agents.core.voice.listening module (a test pins it, since core.X and agents.core.X load as different modules here). The push to the desktop shell: frontend/src/listening.ts follows the stream with the HUD's own token (EventSource cannot send one) and falls back to polling every 5 s; it combines the hub's state with the window's own mic (useVoice active counts as listening), and only inside the desktop app forwards the loudest to the shell (setDesktopListening in frontend/src/desktop.tsx sends only changes and re-sends after a refused call; a closing window sends off). The indicator surface is the tray icon (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs): a desktop_listening command, allowed only to the local HUD windows (verify, capability allow-desktop-listening, build.rs manifest), records each window's state on a board and shows the loudest (desktop/src-tauri/src/indicator.rs): a menu-bar title while a mic is open (● listening, ○ wake word) — shown on macOS and next to the icon on Linux, not on Windows — and a tooltip naming the state on macOS and Windows. It is read-only: nothing on the tray or in the command can open or close a mic. Tests: tests/test_h222_listening.py (44), including the capture's state trail, every early end, a failing transcription, authenticated, unauthenticated and anonymous satellites and a satellite dropping mid-capture, the stream framing, the routes and guard, the shell's command, permission and capability, and the rustc unit tests of indicator.rs; src/test/listening.test.tsx (8) and src/test/desktop-listening.test.ts (3). 65 mutants: 64 killed after five cases were added; the other was a redundant check, removed. The desktop crate was not compiled here: its tray calls were checked against the Tauri 2.11.5 source. Named speech compatibility review: listening state, GET-only snapshot/stream and mic authority are unchanged; listening regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/listening.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/pipeline.py) [d3](../agents/core/voice/wake_word.py) |

| <a id="h227"></a>H227 | "What can it do right now" inspector: live status line plus a session panel of tools, skills, resolved system prompt and MCP servers | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Hermes' "what can it do right now" view is here: one read-only answer, for one agent as seen by one principal, built from the code a turn runs and served on three surfaces. agents/core/inspector.py holds it; build_inspector (:108) returns up to five sections (SECTIONS, :63). (1) Views. VIEWS (:56) names the five tool-profile postures: the owner and a guest on the HUD (owner, guest), the owner and a stranger on an external channel (inbound-owner, inbound; Telegram stands for every channel, under the inbound action origin a real channel turn has) and no human (internal). The look binds that principal and origin in a task of its own (:125), so nothing it binds, and nothing the runtime notes while resolving the offer, outlives it. (2) status (_status, :155): version, the backend and model the router serves, the local model's state through project_llm_status, the transcript budget llm.tool_loop_context_tokens (0 reads as 75 % of the model window), the tool loop and safe mode. (3) tools (_tools, :194): the tool runtime's own resolved offer for the agent under that principal (_resolve_offer: registry or the capability registry's view, job toolset, posture, the agent's tools: list, the shared-session rule), not the raw registry. Each row says whether the tool is gated and whether its output is fenced as untrusted, with the description cut to DESCRIPTION_CHARS (160, :65). The withheld names are listed, a resolver failure offers nothing, and loop_enabled says whether the model sees any of them (with the loop off they are what the posture would offer). (4) skills (_skills, :215): the rows prompt_catalog gives the prompt under that principal and that offer, or none when llm.skills_in_prompt is off. (5) mcp (_mcp, :225): each server's transport, trust tier, transport-aware liveness (is_connected, so an HTTP server reads connected; a probe that raises is down) and visible tools, with no URL or command line. (6) system_prompt (_prompt, :253): the agent's system prompt and the user part Agent.build_prompt sends for an empty user message: persona, agent context, the core-memory block, the runtime, language and grounding rails and the skills block. tokens (:266) estimates what that fixed part costs every turn before history. The texts are masked by the log redactor (SecretRedactionFilter; nothing is shown when it cannot load, _redactor :73) and capped together at PROMPT_CAP_BYTES (64 KiB, :64) on a character boundary. The core block is read with freeze=False (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3198, :3258, :3338): the frozen block when a turn froze one, else a fresh render that is not kept, so a look never fixes the day's snapshot. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/admin/inspector?agent=&view=&section= (agents/core/routers/status.py:123, admin-guarded; 404 for an unknown agent, 400 naming the views or sections, 503 before the hub is up). nerva inspect \[agent\] \[--as VIEW\] \[--section tools\|skills\|prompt\|mcp\|status\] \[--json\] (cmd_inspect, agents/cli/nerva.py:1025; render_inspector :1046) prints the same payload section by section, or as it came. The HUD's Inspector panel (frontend/src/panels/inspector.tsx, Console → Admin) reads the same route with a status line, an As switch and folding sections. Tests: tests/test_inspector.py (43) builds a partial orchestrator over the coordinator's real ToolRPC registry. It covers the guest's tools equal to ToolProfileResolver's set and not the registry, every view's posture and bindings, the agent's list, the marks and the cut, the loop off, a failing resolver, an unwired runtime, the skills, the prompt (skills block, core memory, rails, empty message, tokens), the unfrozen core block, masking, the withheld prompt, the 64 KiB cap on a split character, no leaked binding, MCP liveness, the status line and safe mode, section order, the route (payload, defaults, refusals, admin token, guard snapshot, 503) and the CLI (--json equal to the payload, rendering, --section, the loop-off line, the tree, one list of views and sections). frontend/src/panels/inspector.test.tsx has 4. 82 mutants: 79 caught, ten after survivors got cases. One redundant reset was simplified away. The origin passed to classify_turn is equivalent, because each view's principal alone decides the surface. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h242"></a>H242 | Workspace/project model, repo discovery, strict setup verification and diagnostics bundle | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:296) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:145, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:334-343), and (H380) unless its provider refuses the key that route would use; a provider that says nothing about the key reads unknown, and a 429 stays runnable. A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:536). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:283), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:432-436). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set and the admin token only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address (scripts/doctor.py:400-407, scripts/doctor.py:353), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:483-487). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:463-468; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:625); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:635). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:181, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:183). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:56). An end-to-end test drives both terminal checks against the real app (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:593). Diagnostics stay local: the redacted bundle is served over one admin GET (agents/core/routers/support.py:21), and neither module imports a network client (tests/test_support_bundle.py:67), so the share_nous upload stays refused. The projects/workspaces model was skipped by decision. Known limits the row does not require: the verdict is for the route a short Jarvis chat takes; and a cloud route is judged by the router's own selection, not by a live call. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h283"></a>H283 | Know what kind of machine this is, and tell the model | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub now speaks systemd's sd_notify, and the operator can tell the model what kind of machine this is. Host detection was already richer than Hermes' (probe_host, agents/core/host_probe.py:406; detect_hardware, agents/core/hardware.py:215; /healthz and /readyz, agents/core/routers/ops.py:42 and :149), but nothing wrote to NOTIFY_SOCKET and the model was told only the live backend. agents/core/sd_notify.py: notify (agents/core/sd_notify.py:66) sends one datagram to NOTIFY_SOCKET, a path or an @abstract name (_address, agents/core/sd_notify.py:48), and never raises (a socket nobody listens on is a debug line); with NOTIFY_SOCKET unset (a shell, Docker, launchd, Windows) nothing is sent (enabled, agents/core/sd_notify.py:61). serve.py runs sd_notify.NotifyingServer (agents/core/sd_notify.py:159), a uvicorn.Server subclass: after the port is bound its startup calls NOTIFIER.ready with /readyz's own body (Notifier.ready, agents/core/sd_notify.py:129), so READY=1 follows the bind and a failed bind is never announced. The lifespan sends nothing, because uvicorn runs it before binding, and a raw \`uvicorn agents.web:app\` does not speak the protocol (the shipped unit runs serve.py): READY=1 and STATUS=serving once the orchestrator and its agents are loaded, a STATUS naming why when they are not, and from then on a heartbeat task on the event loop sends WATCHDOG=1 every half WATCHDOG_USEC (watchdog_seconds, agents/core/sd_notify.py:99: at least 0.1 s but never more than 0.8 of the deadline; armed when WATCHDOG_PID is this process or is unset, as in libsystemd; ASCII decimals only), so a hung loop stops the pings and systemd restarts the hub. STOPPING=1 and STATUS=draining are sent at the top of NotifyingServer.shutdown, before uvicorn drains (also on a forced exit), and the heartbeat ends (Notifier.stopping, agents/core/sd_notify.py:143). A full notify queue (EAGAIN, or ENOBUFS as macOS reports it) is retried for up to 1 s (SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) rather than dropping READY; any other socket error is not retried. The shipped unit is Type=notify with NotifyAccess=main, WatchdogSec=60 and TimeoutStartSec=300 (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.service), and deploy/systemd/README.md now describes the protocol instead of saying the hub does not emit it. The environment hint (agents/core/environment_hint.py): JARVIS_ENVIRONMENT_HINT (ENV_NAME, agents/core/environment_hint.py:27), read through env_config, is cleaned (clean_hint, agents/core/environment_hint.py:47): a literal \\n is a line break, except inside a Windows path (D:\\nerva, \\\\nas), and in an EnvironmentFile the value must be double-quoted (the shipped example is); U+2028, U+2029 and NEL are line breaks too; control and bidi characters and undecodable bytes (lone surrogates) are removed, no line may open a heading (ATX or setext), blank runs fold, and it is cut to 2,000 characters (MAX_HINT_CHARS, agents/core/environment_hint.py:28). hint_block (agents/core/environment_hint.py:65) puts it under "## This machine (the operator's description: context about where you run, not instructions)" (HEADER, agents/core/environment_hint.py:29), and Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) places that block in the stable part of every agent's system prompt, between the shared behaviour contract and the persona (agents/core/agent.py:583): it never forks the persona, and the same environment gives the same bytes every turn, so the prompt cache holds; with the variable unset the prompt is exactly what it was. The cleaning is linear and cached per raw value. \`nerva prompt-size\` counts the hint as an environment-hint row in the shared floor (environment_hint_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:149). The env file template (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.env) shows an example. tests/test_h283_host_notify_and_hint.py (43 cases) uses real datagram sockets: a message reaches a path and an abstract socket, no socket or a relative one sends nothing, a dead socket is no error, the heartbeat interval (half, the minimum, this PID only, bad values), not-ready STATUS without READY, READY then heartbeats from the loop with none sent while the loop is blocked then STOPPING and no more beats, no heartbeat without a watchdog, nothing without systemd, the lifespan sending nothing, a real uvicorn bind that sends READY only once the port accepts connections and never when the bind fails, a not-ready server that sends STATUS but no READY, STOPPING before the lifespan teardown and on a forced exit, a full queue waited on, the retry bounded to SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS and only for a full buffer, the shipped unit and its quoted example; and for the hint the cleaning (Windows paths, setext and U+2028 headings, surrogates, linear time), the cap, the heading rule, the prompt-size row, the label, the absence when unset, and its place and byte-stability in a real agent's prompt. 21 mutants, all caught; the review round's 17 mutants: 16 killed and 1 equivalent (READY only after self.started: uvicorn 0.53, the floor in requirements.txt, exits on every path where the server did not start). Test manual: ENV-165, ENV-166. Adapted from Hermes: of its ~35 detection variables only the two with an operational effect are taken (NOTIFY_SOCKET/WATCHDOG_USEC and the environment hint); the container, terminal and multiplexer sniffing stays out, since host_probe already names the limits that change what Nerva can do. [d1](../agents/core/sd_notify.py) [d2](../agents/core/environment_hint.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h326"></a>H326 | Model-facing skill discovery (prompt index + progressive disclosure) | copy | De reverificat | Dovadă schimbată | Dovada s-a schimbat sau lipsește; reevaluează înainte de a număra rândul ca terminat. [d1](../agents/core/skills/loader.py) [d2](../agents/core/skills/tools.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |
| <a id="h327"></a>H327 | SKILL.md frontmatter contract (agentskills.io-compatible) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The SKILL.md frontmatter contract Hermes parses (agent/skill_utils.parse_frontmatter and tools/skills_tool_plugin.py) now lives in one shared module, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py, used by both the loader and the importer. Parsing. split_frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:47): - drops a leading UTF-8 BOM before the fence check (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:56); - takes the closing fence at column 0 only, as Hermes' \`\\n---\\s\*\\n\` does, so an indented \`---\` inside a block scalar is content (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:63); - falls back to key: value lines for a block that is not valid YAML (_naive_key_values, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:83), instead of demoting the file to the heading dialect. The fallback is stricter than Hermes' line split: - only column-0 keys count, so a nested \`name:\` cannot replace the skill's own; - a value wrapped in matching quotes loses them; - a fallback that finds neither a name nor a description counts as no frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:77), so a heading-dialect file framed by fences keeps its heading parse. Identity decisions pass lenient=False (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:72), so a name only the fallback can find never decides one: the Hermes pin gate (agents/core/skills/importer.py:271) and the migration and rescan slugs (agents/core/skills/importer.py:1192, agents/core/skills/importer.py:1376). SkillLoader._parse_manifest decodes with utf-8-sig on both the snapshot and the path read (agents/core/skills/loader.py:854, agents/core/skills/loader.py:856). A manifest the normaliser cannot read registers under the directory name instead of failing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:871). The manifest: - The name is capped at 64 characters and stripped after the cut (agents/core/skills/loader.py:890; the caps are at agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:28-29). - The description is capped at 1024 characters with an ellipsis, quotes kept (cap_description, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:351). When none is declared, it is the first non-heading body line, as Hermes' listing reads it (agents/core/skills/loader.py:892-893). - A list-valued author or license is joined (agents/core/skills/loader.py:895). - agents and requires read a YAML list, \`\[a, b\]\` or \`a, b\` as plain strings (agents/core/skills/loader.py:882-883). - Command entries are reduced to JSON types (agents/core/skills/loader.py:967). contract_fields (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:359), merged into every frontmatter manifest (agents/core/skills/loader.py:902), keeps the keys Hermes recognises: - homepage and compatibility; - platforms, read as Hermes matches them (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:204): a list, or one string as one name. Each name maps through PLATFORM_MAP and matches the OS whose sys.platform starts with it, so darwin and win count and osx and win64 do not. An unmatched name is kept, so a gate refuses it everywhere, as Hermes hides it; - environments and requires_apps, where a string is one item (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:224, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:234); - triggers and dependencies; - tags and related_skills, where a declared metadata.hermes value wins over the top-level key (skill_view's \`hermes_meta.get(k) or frontmatter.get(k)\`); - setup, prerequisites and required_credential_files; - required_environment_variables (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:256), which matches Hermes' _get_required_environment_variables: it merges the declared list, setup.collect_secrets and the legacy prerequisites.env_vars, first entry wins, names are validated, and \`optional\` is read for truth; - metadata.hermes.{tags, category, upstream_skill, supersedes, session_platforms, requires_toolsets, requires_tools, fallback_for_toolsets, fallback_for_tools, config} (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:340). A config entry needs a key and a description. Skill exposes platforms, environments, requires_apps, required_env (names only; no value is read) and hermes_meta, and to_dict carries them (agents/core/skills/loader.py:476, agents/core/skills/loader.py:524, agents/core/skills/loader.py:532, agents/core/skills/loader.py:536, agents/core/skills/loader.py:542). Imported frontmatter is third-party text: - json_safe (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:128) bounds depth, width, string length and total nodes, with a 2,048-node budget per value (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:134). A 2 KB YAML alias bomb that would expand to 64^6 nodes parses in about 0.02 s. - Dates become ISO strings, and NaN, infinities and integers past 64 bits become null or "". A hex integer past Python's str() limit no longer raises (_scalar_text, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:112), so the security-posture route serialises Skill.to_dict with allow_nan=False. - discover() skips a skill whose load raises and keeps the rest, while the signing misconfiguration still stops discovery (agents/core/skills/loader.py:662-667). - A second skill taking an occupied registry name is logged: a warning within one tree (e.g. two names sharing their first 64 characters), info for a user skill replacing a bundled one (agents/core/skills/loader.py:679). Deliberate differences from Hermes: - A closing fence at end of file with no trailing newline is accepted, as Nerva always did. - The registry is last-writer-wins with that log line; Hermes' listing keeps the first. Tests: tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py has 51 cases, including the alias bomb (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:286), a hex integer in seven fields (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:306), NaN and huge numbers through to_dict (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:315), one broken skill among others (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:328), the fallback rules (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:354), Hermes platform matching (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:406), the strict identity parse (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:434) and the snapshot BOM path (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:482). A mutation pass over 25 mutants (first-wins, precedence, the depth, width and node caps, both utf-8-sig decodes, the fence, the fallback rules, optional, the strict gate, NaN and big ints, the discover guard, the collision log, the platform string, author join, quotes, the body-line fallback, name strip, command sanitising, requires_apps) killed all of them. 454 tests across 28 skill, import, marketplace, migration and posture files pass. Gating on these fields is not this row: H328 (visibility) and the setup rows consume them. | <a id="h344"></a>H344 | Reading skills from directories shared with other AI tools | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Import-once stays the only way a Hermes / OpenClaw / Claude Code skill enters Nerva's skills tree (no live-scanned external_dirs tier; SkillLoader.discover() is unchanged), and a re-run of \`python scripts/nerva_import.py\` now notices upstream movement instead of reporting a blanket skipped/exists. Each imported skill is re-detected against the absolute source_path and sha256 recorded in its manifest.json (importer.py:955, importer.py:1000): unchanged; changed, with the old and new digests plus a bounded line diff shown by --json; moved to another folder of the same install and followed (importer.py:934); removed; renamed; unreadable; or source_untrusted when a record names a file outside the install, which is then never read (importer.py:1290). The default run is a dry run. \`--apply --update\` re-imports only changed skills that this source imported (importer.py:1065): the current copy is backed up under data/imports/skill_backups, PENDING_REVIEW is written before the new bytes land, SKILL.sig and the legacy approval marker are cleared, and the owner's approval is revoked. A linked control file, an unusable backup dir or a refused save fails closed, and the write is bound to the probed digest. Every import and re-import crosses skill.install through the real Action Kernel at the shipped CLI (nerva_import.py:577, bound at nerva_import.py:1044 and as the ImportRunner default at nerva_import.py:682), behind SKILL_IMPORT_CONTRACT (nerva_import.py:145). The persisted kill switch is re-read on every decision, so an engaged halt refuses an import or re-import before a byte moves. The external origin keeps a policy GRANT at QUEUE, and only GRANT or QUEUE writes (nerva_import.py:764), so every write lands quarantined. The human summary names a local edit that --update replaces (with its backup path), a failed revoke, and moved, untrusted and refused rows (nerva_import.py:938). Tests in test_nerva_import.py: 44 passed and 1 live-gated skip under python 3.11 and 3.12. They include test_cli_skill_writes_cross_the_real_kernel_and_honour_the_kill_switch, test_runner_default_authorizer_is_the_kernel_for_skills_and_queue_for_memory, test_source_moved_within_the_install_is_followed_not_reported_removed, test_apply_update_reimports_changed_skill_into_quarantine_and_revokes_approval, test_rescan_never_follows_a_record_outside_the_install and test_reimport_refuses_a_linked_control_file_and_never_writes_through_it. Two known limits sit outside the accepted requirement. (1) The CLI binds no durable audit sink, so each kernel decision is recorded only on its report row (kernel/kernel_reason) and in the in-process kernel metrics. (2) A record written before this change with a cwd-relative source_path (relative --home) is not rewritten by an unchanged re-run. From another directory it reads skipped/exists plus source_untrusted until a changed source is re-imported with --apply --update, which records the absolute path. | <a id="h368"></a>H368 | Refuse to speak the wrong protocol to a lookalike host | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A hostname-parsed protocol table guards every model backend at the wire. host_mandated_protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:112) takes the hostname from hostname_of (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:85). hostname_of parses strings with httpx.URL, the parser the wire dials with (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:103). A fullwidth, ideographic or halfwidth-ideographic dot spelling of a vendor host is therefore refused at HybridRouter.detect() as well as at the wire. urlsplit remains the fallback for text httpx rejects, and httpx cannot dial that text either. The host is lower-cased with the trailing dot dropped, then matched exactly or as whole labels (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:62-69): api.anthropic.com -\> anthropic_messages; api.openai.com and one regional label -\> openai; bedrock-runtime\[-fips\].\<region\>.amazonaws.com\[.cn\] -\> bedrock_converse. Lookalike suffixes, path spoofs and userinfo spoofs mandate nothing. Each backend's ledger name declares its protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:49). Every llm_async_client caller on this head is declared: anthropic, gemini (gemini_cache included), openai-responses, xai, openrouter, lm-studio, ollama, vlm and the approval judge's openai-compatible. The synchronous embedding clients (llm_sync_client and llm_sync_request_hook, agents/core/llm/egress.py:110-137: ollama, lm-studio) run the same protocol_refusal check. protocol_refusal (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:131) also refuses an undeclared backend on a mandated host. The check lives in the egress-ledger request hook that every backend's llm_async_client carries (agents/core/llm/egress.py:76-79), so it runs on every redirect hop. A mismatch records an allowed=False ledger row with the reason and raises before the transport is touched, so neither the body nor the auth header leaves. HybridRouter.detect refuses a compatible base URL on a host that mandates another protocol (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:376); the refusal is logged and the backend is not built. HostProtocolRefused subclasses Exception, neither OSError nor httpx.HTTPError (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:73), so no connection-failure handler reads a refusal as 'backend down'. OllamaBackend.resolve_context_window logs a warning and returns unknown on a refusal (agents/core/llm/base.py:768), instead of raising out of compaction and route planning. Adaptation from Hermes: Nerva has no per-session api_mode that can go stale, so the mandate refuses a mismatched backend rather than re-picking a protocol. Nerva's OpenAI family admits both OpenAI dialects (chat completions and Responses) at api.openai.com. Base URLs remain env-only until the named-custom-endpoints row exists, and the wire guard covers any base URL unchanged. Coverage is tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py (43 tests): - vendor hosts, including the 5 IDNA-dot spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:61); - 15 lookalike, path, userinfo and other hosts (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:82); - an OpenAI-protocol backend aimed at api.anthropic.com sends zero requests and leaves two blocked ledger rows (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:119); - the client raises before dispatch (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:141); - matching and unmandated hosts still flow (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:151); - a redirect onto a vendor host is refused per hop (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:164); - router refusals, including both IDNA spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:204, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:213, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:234); - the default and custom endpoints are still built (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:222); - the refusal is neither an OSError nor an httpx.HTTPError (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:247); - the Ollama probe degrades and leaves a blocked ledger row (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:254). All wire tests use the real client, with httpx.MockTransport injected through llm_async_client's public transport kwarg. Parameter-repair source review (2026-10-02): _post_chat still uses the existing declared LM Studio client and physical request hooks for every send. Model-unloaded recovery and one scoped temperature repair cannot change the host or wire protocol. Host-protocol and real auxiliary send regressions pass; no capability promotion.
