| <a id="h373"></a>H373 | See how much provider quota is left before hitting the wall | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now reads each provider's quota off every cloud response and shares a 429 with every process. agents/core/llm/quota.py holds the rules. llm_async_client, the one constructor every cloud backend's HTTP client goes through (Anthropic, Gemini, OpenRouter, xAI, the Responses dialect, VLM), now hangs two hooks (agents/core/llm/egress.py:97-98). response_hook (agents/core/llm/quota.py:338) reads the provider's own quota headers with parse_headers (agents/core/llm/quota.py:146): OpenAI-style x-ratelimit-{limit,remaining,reset}-{requests,tokens}; Anthropic's anthropic-ratelimit-{requests,tokens,input-tokens,output-tokens}-{limit,remaining,reset}; and retry-after as seconds or an HTTP date read as UTC. It understands resets given as durations ("6m0s", "20ms") or RFC 3339 times, and it stores the latest numbers per backend, key and model. On a 429 it blocks that backend, key and model (request_model, agents/core/llm/quota.py:78: Gemini's path, else the JSON body's model), or the whole key when the request names no model, until the provider's retry-after, else 30 s, never over 900 s (QuotaStore.block, agents/core/llm/quota.py:207). request_hook (agents/core/llm/quota.py:317) runs before the egress recorder on every request. It refuses a request whose model or whole key is held with ProviderRateLimited before it is sent, so a refused request never reaches the provider or the egress ledger. Another key in the pool is not held: on a refusal the Anthropic backend (_post_messages, and the stream, which retries a refused open on the next key) and the Gemini backend (generate, tool turn, stream) fail over to the next key as on a 429, and ProviderRateLimited carries retry_in, so AuthProfilePool.report_failure(key, cooldown=retry_in) keeps the held key cool for its whole hold (agents/core/llm/anthropic.py:98, agents/core/llm/gemini.py:204, agents/core/llm/auth_rotation.py:159). The H380 provider probe reports a held key as rate_limited, sends nothing, and caches that verdict no longer than the hold and at most 30 s (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:191). Retry-after and reset values must be finite ('inf', 'nan' and overflowing numbers are unreadable), a negative retry-after is unreadable so the 30 s default applies, and a reset time without a zone is read as UTC. The store drops its pre-model tables once. A local host is never tracked or held, even when the same backend is held in the cloud. The state lives in one SQLite file under the data folder (QuotaStore, agents/core/llm/quota.py:173), so the hub, the CLI, the scheduler and any worker see the same numbers and the same holds. A key is known only by a 12-hex fingerprint of its hash, and every failure is swallowed, so a quota reader never breaks a request. usage (agents/core/llm/quota.py:361) gives each backend's requests and tokens left, the fraction left (clamped, none for a missing or non-positive limit), time to reset and any hold. It is served by GET /api/llm/quota (agents/core/routers/models_llm.py:439, admin, 503 quota_unavailable when unreadable or when a row cannot be serialised; each row carries its model, which /usage and the panel name), the HUD Provider Quota panel (frontend/src/panels/provider-quota.tsx, Admin group: bars, numbers, reset times, a hold tag), and /usage (agents/core/commands.py:225, admin tier, rendered by render). The router is not fed a forecast; Hermes' own guard is likewise a refusal before the request, which is what this does. tests/conftest.py gives each test its own quota store, so a mocked 429 never holds a later test (tests/conftest.py:187). tests/test_h373_provider_quota.py (78 cases) covers durations, retry-after (seconds, dates, no zone under another host timezone), OpenAI and Anthropic headers, unreadable values, key fingerprints, and the store: latest numbers, clamping, a negative limit, blocks with retry-after, the default and cap, a hold before numbers, two processes, damaged data and the default path. It also covers the hooks on a real llm_async_client (recording, no headers, a 429 hold refused before dialling and absent from the egress ledger, another key, the default hold, other errors, a local model, a local request of a held backend, a failing store), the local-host test, the hook order, rendering, the admin route and the admin /usage command. The review round added the fail-over on a refusal for both backends and their streams, the cooldown floor, per-model holds, finite and signed header values, zone-less resets, the route's serialisation, and the probe's held key (tests/test_h380_provider_probe.py). frontend/src/test/provider-quota-panel.test.tsx has 3 cases. There are 50 mutants, all killed; the review round's 18 mutants, all killed (5 by tests added after a first pass). | <a id="h380"></a>H380 | Prove a configured cloud provider actually works | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A configured cloud key is checked with its provider before a turn finds out. agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py asks each cloud provider once, with the key the hub would use (configured_key, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:87: for Anthropic and Gemini, the only providers with an AuthProfilePool (POOLED), the first of the \`\<VAR\>S\` pool split on commas and whitespace as the router splits it, else \`\<VAR\>\`; every other provider reads \`\<VAR\>\` only). The command center asks with the key the route's backend holds: the pool's current key for Claude and Gemini, the compatible backend's own api_key otherwise. It sends one authenticated GET (request_for, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:105): Anthropic /v1/models with x-api-key and anthropic-version, Gemini /v1beta/models with x-goog-api-key, OpenRouter \`\<base\>/key\` (its model list is public; the key record answers 401 to a key it does not know), and \`\<base\>/models\` with a bearer for the other OpenAI-shaped providers, honouring a configured base URL. The GET goes through llm_async_client, so it is in the egress ledger as \`llm:\<provider\>\`, and H368's host-protocol mandate refuses a mismatch before it leaves; the xAI and OpenAI Responses probes use trust_env=False, as their backends do (NO_ENV_TRUST, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:67). Nothing is generated. The verdict (probe, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:223) is one of: \`ok\` (any 2xx, with the listed model count); \`no_listing\` (404/405: nothing at that URL, so the key is not proven); \`auth_failed\` (401, or a 400 whose body names a bad key: Gemini API_KEY_INVALID/API_KEY_EXPIRED, xAI "Incorrect API key"; the body is read inside the probe and never returned); \`forbidden\` (403); \`rate_limited\` (429, or a key held by the shared 429 guard of H373, with nothing sent); \`error\` (any other status, which is named, or an unexpected failure); \`unreachable\` (any transport error); \`refused\` (the host mandate); \`not_configured\` (no key, nothing sent); or \`not_cloud\` (a local provider). Only \`ok\` proves the key (WORKING, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:61). The key and the response body are never in a result or a log line. A result is cached per provider, key fingerprint and probed URL, so a changed key or base URL is probed afresh: for TTL_SECONDS (300 s), and a transient verdict (rate_limited, error, unreachable) for TRANSIENT_TTL_SECONDS (30 s) or, for a guard-held key, no longer than its hold (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:53-56). Concurrent callers, forced ones included, share one in-flight request (_fresh, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:205). A forced re-check inside MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS (30 s) answers the cached verdict marked \`throttled\`. last (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:263) reads the cache without a request. probe_all (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:301) asks every cloud provider concurrently, except one whose key variable belongs to a base URL on another host (_key_for_elsewhere, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:287): an OPENAI_API_KEY beside an OPENAI_BASE_URL on a gateway is not sent to api.openai.com unless llm.compatible_provider is openai-responses. POST /api/admin/llm/providers/probe (admin; admin_llm_providers_probe, agents/core/routers/admin.py:971) checks one provider or all, with \`force\`; an unknown provider is a 404. The Console's Provider Check panel (Admin; frontend/src/panels/provider-check.tsx) probes nothing until asked. It shows each verdict (\`key rejected\` with the HTTP status beside it; \`key not verified (nothing at this URL)\` in amber for no_listing), the model count and a named HTTP status, offers a forced re-check per provider, and says when a re-check was throttled. The command-center model block asks the provider for a selected cloud route (_cloud_probe, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:224; agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:339) and waits at most CLOUD_PROBE_WAIT_SECONDS (4 s), after which the verdict reads \`unreachable\` and the probe finishes in the background. The route is ready (\`cloud_selected\`) on \`ok\`; \`rate_limited\` keeps it runnable (\`cloud_rate_limited\`); \`no_listing\`, \`error\` and \`unreachable\` make it unknown (ready=None, \`cloud_no_listing\` / \`cloud_error\` / \`cloud_unreachable\`); \`auth_failed\`, \`forbidden\`, \`refused\` and \`not_configured\` make it not runnable (CLOUD_PROBE_REASONS, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:204). The block carries \`cloud_probe\` (provider, verdict, status code, time, cached; agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:370), and its hint names the provider, what it said and the HTTP status (_model_hint, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:391). \`nerva doctor\`'s runtime_resolves and \`nerva status\`'s runnable line read that block, so a wrong or revoked key now fails them with its reason instead of passing on "a key is set". tests/test_h380_provider_probe.py (30 cases) covers the request per provider with the key only in a header, the key resolution, one read and its model count across list shapes, nine statuses, three transport failures and an unexpected one, the protocol refusal recorded as not sent, the egress row, no request without a key or for a local provider, no key or body in results or logs, the cache per key with expiry, the throttled and then allowed forced re-check, the cache-only read, probe_all, the admin route (admin-only, one, all, force, 404) and the command center's key choice. tests/test_h380b_provider_probe_review.py (35 cases) covers the review round: OpenRouter asked about its key, not its public list; a gateway key kept away from api.openai.com and OpenAI asked when the key is its own; a 400 that names a bad key; a missing listing that proves nothing; the key read as the router reads it and the compatible backend's own key; a guard-held key rate_limited for the hold; concurrent callers sharing one request; the bounded wait; readiness per verdict and the short cache of a transient one; trust_env matching the backends; the hint; and the cache slot per URL. tests/test_first_run_command_center.py covers every \`cloud_\*\` reason and \`cloud_probe\` in the model block. The panel has 3 vitest cases. The row's 38 mutants were all killed. The review round's 36 mutants: 29 caught at once, six after six cases were added to tests/test_h380b_provider_probe_review.py (a finished request is not joined again, a caller that stops waiting leaves the verdict cached, a request in flight on another event loop is not awaited, the cache-only read forgets a transient verdict, reset forgets a request in flight, the hint for an unknown provider), and one equivalent (\`ready is True\` and truthiness agree on True/False/None). [d1](../agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/onboarding.py) |

| <a id="h409"></a>H409 | Outbound signed lifecycle webhooks | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The whole accepted delivery path is missing: (1) an admin-managed registry of outbound subscribers (URL, secret, event filter) with an operator entry point (admin route plus a \`nerva\` CLI verb or HUD control). (2) A delivery worker subscribed to the same event catalogue as ExtensionEventBus that POSTs the allowlisted JSON payload with an HMAC signature header from compute_signature. (3) Bounded retries with backoff and an explicit no-follow redirect policy. (4) Egress through the plugin.egress kernel mediation (agents/core/kernel/registry.py:45) and an estop check, so a kill-switch or budget stop silences the feed. (5) Payloads passed through the redaction engine before they leave the box. The reaction/vibe detector is excluded by the rationale. [d1](../agents/core/webhooks.py) [d2](../agents/core/extensions/events.py) [d3](../agents/core/extensions/manifest.py) |
| <a id="h410"></a>H410 | Universal secret redaction on every log record | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Every log record, from every process, now goes through two filters: H495's shape scanner and a catalogue of credential-bearing names plus the Romanian identifiers. agents/core/log_catalogue.py (outside security/, additive) is a scanner over the names that make a value a credential whatever it looks like: query parameters (QUERY_PARAMS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:54: access_token, refresh_token, id_token, token, auth, api_key, client_secret, secret, password, signature/sig, the AWS presign parameters, session, jwt), masked at any length up to the next separator; body, form and structured-log keys (SECRET_KEYS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:62), quoted in JSON or a Python repr, assigned with a quoted value, or unquoted when the value is six characters or more; the Authorization header after any scheme (Bearer, Basic, Token, Digest, ApiKey, HMAC…), or without one when the value is twelve characters or more and carries a digit, so \`Authorization: denied\` stays; the credential headers X-Api-Key, X-Auth-Token, X-Access-Token, X-Goog-Api-Key, X-User-Token, X-Admin-Token, Api-Key and X-Webhook-Token (SECRET_HEADERS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:68); and the whole value of Cookie and Set-Cookie. It also runs PIIScanner's own CNP and IBAN patterns (agents/core/security/scanner.py:287-288) with their checksum validators, and an IBAN written in spaced groups of four; the other PII patterns stay out (an e-mail or phone number in a log is often the point of the line; card and SSN shapes carry no checksum). A bare \`key\` parameter (settings routes use it for a setting's name) and a bare \`token:\` key (token counts) are not in the catalogue. The rules are in _rules (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:91); a value already masked is never re-masked, so a JWT the first filter named keeps its name. CatalogueScanner (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:126) offers redact, scan and the _compiled rows H495's prefilter is built from, and CatalogueRedactionFilter (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:161) runs it through an unchanged SecretRedactionFilter (agents/core/security/log_redaction.py:230): the arguments uvicorn's AccessFormatter unpacks are kept, a secret split between the format string and an argument masks the argument whole and is named after the finding that holds, tracebacks and stack info are masked, a failing scanner drops the record's content to \[redaction-unavailable\], and the one JARVIS_LOG_REDACTION switch, snapshotted at import, turns both filters off. It wraps rather than subclasses, so H495's installer, which skips a handler already carrying a SecretRedactionFilter, never mistakes it for its own. install_catalogue_redaction_everywhere (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:220) covers the root handlers and every logger's own (uvicorn's detached ones included), idempotently, and counts and names a handler that refuses the filter. core.log's _install_redaction (agents/core/log.py:69) installs it right after the H495 filter, so setup_logging (agents/core/log.py:108) gives every handler both, in that order; install_redaction (agents/core/log.py:102) is the same for a process that configures its own logging. The two entry points that called a bare basicConfig and wrote every record unredacted now call it: the headless coordinator (configure_logging, scripts/coordinator.py:145) and the MCP stdio bridge (configure_logging, scripts/nerva_mcp_stdio.py:149, still stderr only, since stdout is its protocol channel). Neither takes setup_logging's rotating file handler, which a second process would rotate under the hub (critic note 32). The H145 log reader runs the catalogue on every line it serves (agents/core/log_tail.py:167), for lines written before it or by a process it did not cover. The test isolation fixture strips the catalogue filter with the H495 one (tests/conftest.py:271). The H495 module's docstring (agents/core/security/, a protected path) still names the coordinator and the MCP bridge as uncovered and says no PII pass runs; it describes that module alone. tests/test_h410_log_catalogue.py (52 cases) covers the five probes the audit found unmasked and every other name in the catalogue, ordinary text that must stay (a failed authorization, a setting key, an exit code, a token count, a 13-digit order number, an IBAN-shaped string that fails its checksum, a short value), the exact line kept around a mask, CNP and IBAN compact and spaced, an access-log record keeping its five arguments, a traceback, a JWT keeping the first filter's name, a straddling IBAN, the switch, a failing scanner, both filters on every handler once and in order, a refusing handler counted and named, both entry points with a fresh root (the bridge writing nothing to stdout), and the log reader. 31 mutants: 30 killed (4 by cases added after a first pass) and 1 equivalent that removed a redundant \`(?:proxy-)?\` prefix (\`\\b\` holds after the hyphen). Test manual: SEC-216. [d1](../agents/core/log_catalogue.py) [d2](../agents/core/log.py) [d3](../agents/core/log_tail.py) |

| <a id="h441"></a>H441 | Getting back into a conversation: continue, resume, and a free recap | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Getting back into a conversation now comes with a free recap, rendered locally from the stored turns and never by a model, on resume, as /recap in every channel and in the HUD. render_recap (agents/core/memory/recap.py:80) groups a session's turns into exchanges (an owner turn and the replies that follow it), keeps the last 10 by default (DEFAULT_EXCHANGES, agents/core/memory/recap.py:24; at most 50, MAX_EXCHANGES, agents/core/memory/recap.py:25), and puts each turn on one line of plain text, whitespace folded, control and bidi characters removed and cut to 280 characters with an ellipsis (_clean, agents/core/memory/recap.py:35; the raw text is cut to four times the limit before cleaning, so a huge stored turn costs no more than a short one). A reply's tools collapse to a count and their distinct names, "\[3 tool calls: web_search, web_fetch\]" (tool_line, agents/core/memory/recap.py:57), names only, never arguments or results, and an owner turn never shows one. The answer carries the exchanges as data and the same recap as text lines (● you / ◆ the agent) with "the last n of m exchanges" when some are left out. The tools had to be recorded first: turns persisted only role, content, agent, time and tokens. The orchestrator opens a per-turn collection when an owner turn arrives (turn_tools.begin, agents/core/memory/turn_tools.py:23; agents/core/orchestrator.py:2014 and :2216), the agent's tool-event sink notes each finished call's tool (turn_tools.note, agents/core/memory/turn_tools.py:30, from the sink's record, agents/core/agent.py:903) while still landing every event in the trail, and the reply that closes the turn is stored with the names (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3621) on Turn.tools (Turn, agents/core/memory/conversation.py:36; a stored value that is not a list of names reads as none, _tool_names, agents/core/memory/conversation.py:62), persisted, reloaded at start, on resume and on an explicit session load (session_continuation._load_locked, agents/core/session_continuation.py:285), carried in a continuation's immutable seed (seed_json, agents/core/session_continuation.py:89), and absent from a turn that called none. The collection is a context variable, so concurrent turns in other sessions never mix, and a tool loop run through asyncio.to_thread still lands in its own turn. POST /sessions/resume answers with "recap" beside the last 20 raw turns it always returned (agents/core/routers/sessions.py:197), rendered from the whole stored session. /recap \[n\] (_recap, agents/core/commands.py:375; registered at agents/core/commands.py:408) answers in any admitted channel from that channel's own session (a chat's session is its own, pinned by the channel handler); on the owner's shared session (a widget visitor, an agent-target webhook, an MCP caller, or any channel with memory.cross_channel_sessions on) it answers only the owner and refuses anyone else before reading a turn, with n exchanges (at most 50; anything else, a non-ASCII digit included, is the default ten), and leaves out its own /recap line, recognised by the command parser in any case (/Recap, /recap@bot). The HUD's Sessions panel (SessionsPanel, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:51) now shows the recap after a resume (RecapView, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:30; toolLine, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:23) as text, never markup, names a refused resume and drops the old recap then, and says "resumed \<id\>" when a hub sends none. tests/test_h441_session_recap.py (22 cases) covers the renderer (last exchanges and the count, tool lines, one bounded line, odd input, no model), the collection (terminal events only, names only, concurrent turns and a worker thread, no turn open, a caller's copy), the agent's sink, the turn's tools stored and reloaded at start and on resume, the manager passing them down, the resume route's recap with no model call, /recap in a guest's channel with a count, with nothing and with no memory, /help listing it, and the orchestrator storing a turn's tools; the review round added /recap refused to a widget visitor, a webhook and MCP through the real routes while the owner still gets it, a guest on the shared session refused before anything is read, the /recap line left out in any case, a non-ASCII digit, a huge turn cut before cleaning, and tools kept through prepare_session and a continued session's seed, resume and next turn; frontend/src/test/sessions-recap.test.tsx (3) covers the panel. 28 mutants (24 Python, 4 HUD), all caught, three after their cases were added (an owner turn with tools, a caller's copy of the list, the reload on resume). Test manual: CHT-118 (and CHT-011 names the recap). Adapted from Hermes: no per-terminal breadcrumbs for -c/--continue (they solve a multi-pane CLI problem Nerva does not have), and the recap is served to the HUD and the chat channels rather than drawn as a terminal panel. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h444"></a>H444 | Reading documents into the conversation | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The part this row says is worth copying is missing. A scanned (image-only) PDF extracts to empty or near-empty text, and file_read returns that as a normal successful read, with no coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text. No size ceiling applies to a document: the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned, where Hermes refuses documents over 50 MB. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. The wider format matrix (.ipynb, .xlsx, legacy Office, OpenDocument, .rtf, .epub) and Markdown conversion are not built; the row's rationale does not ask for them. | <a id="h450"></a>H450 | Say when in plain words (schedule syntax) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Plain-words scheduling now covers the four families Hermes accepts that the job store did not. It also no longer turns a one-shot said in words into a daily job. (1) One-shots. nl_schedule.parse_schedule (agents/core/autonomy/nl_schedule.py:196) takes a now and a zone and answers a one-shot as {ok, at (UTC ISO), description} (_parse_one_shot, :120). It accepts a delay: in 30m, in 1h30m, in 2 hours and 5 minutes, in an hour, peste 2 ore, în 10 minute, peste 2 zile (_duration, :85). It accepts an ISO timestamp (2026-10-01 09:00, anchored to the zone, or with Z / ±HH:MM kept). And it accepts a day word with a time (tomorrow at 9, mâine la 9, today at 18:00, azi la 18, once at 9am, o dată la 9, the next 9 o'clock). A one-shot must be at least one minute ahead and within a year (MAX_ONE_SHOT_AHEAD, :29, _one_shot, :101); a passed time, a date without a time, an impossible date, a bad time, and a delay with anything else after it ("in 2 hours at 9") are refused by name. So 2026-10-01 09:00, tomorrow at 9 and once at 9am are no longer armed as the daily cron 0 9 \* \* \*; a bare time with no day word still means daily, as documented. resolve_schedule (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:271) stores a one-shot in the cron column as @at \<UTC ISO\> (ONE_SHOT_PREFIX, :229; is_one_shot, run_at, :233-238). The store anchors times said in words to the scheduler's zone, which the runner binds (_schedule_zone, :990; :1126). schedule_trigger (:249) gives a DateTrigger for it. register arms a date job (:1184). tick fires it in the minute it falls in (:1279). slot_timing gives its one slot and none after (:1347). A one-shot runs once. Job.runnable is false after one attempt (:131). reserve_attempt refuses a second (:1004). _fire_once reserves the one run before any executor, script jobs included, and records a later firing as skipped: "one-shot already ran" (:1495). register_all does not re-arm a spent one-shot. Giving it a new time re-arms it (_reset_attempts, :981), and a rename does not. It stays out of H687's first run even when first_run is asked for (first_run_policy and first_run_decision answer "one-shot", :563, :589), so it never spends its only run early. The confirmation names its time (arm_confirmation, :614), and as_dict carries one_shot and run_at (:157). /remind in 30m \| … works, and its usage says so. (2) Compact intervals and bare durations: every 2h, every 30m, 2h, 30m, la fiecare 2 ore (_COMPACT, _interval, nl_schedule.py:174-177). 120 minutes becomes every 2 hours. An interval a cron cannot keep (every 45 minutes fired at :00 and :45; 7h, 48h) is refused with the divisors to use. (3) Repeat normaliser: normalize_repeat (jobs.py:410) maps forever/unlimited/always/∞/mereu/la nesfârșit to unlimited, once/1x/x1/o dată to 1, twice/de două ori to 2, and Nx/N times/de N ori/N to N (1-10000). Anything else is refused. validate_options applies it, so options.repeat takes the words on every surface. (4) The HUD jobs panel shows a one-shot by its time (once at …, the viewer's clock) instead of @at, and shows a spent one as done (onceAt, frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:30, :62). Tests: tests/test_h450_schedule_words.py (fixed now and Europe/Bucharest zone; store and runner with a fake scheduler; the fallback ticker; script jobs), frontend jobs.test.tsx and jobs-first-run.test.ts. 62 mutants, all caught after fourteen cases were added. One survivor was a real bug: "in 2 hours at 9" read as a daily 9 o'clock; it is now refused. | <a id="h464"></a>H464 | Park a run on real async work instead of poking it | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Not reachable in production yet: - \*\*No process producer (owner decision 1; blocked on H302).\*\* Nothing in the hub starts run-owned background work and registers its pid: register_process has no caller outside tests. The best first producer is \`terminal_run\` with \`background: true\` on the local-host transport. It needs WorkRunLedger.run_for_task; a spawn with start_new_session, bounded log files and a held proc.wait() that records the exit code; a lifetime cap and killpg on cap, ESTOP or stop; a reaper for stopped runs; schema, approval-card and parity updates; and a KillMode in deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.service (today a restart kills the child and the barrier clears as \`exited\`, indistinguishable from success). The owner must say whether the task/hook trigger is an accepted adaptation of Hermes's pid/session barrier, or whether a pid wait must be reachable for "equivalent". - \*\*Pid waits are unusable on macOS/BSD.\*\* There is no start token there, so every pid wait is refused \`pid_unprovable\`. The fix belongs in exec_cache.py (ctypes libproc proc_pidinfo(PROC_PIDTBSDINFO)) or in a producer-held Popen handle, and needs macOS hardware or a macOS CI runner to verify. - \*\*The judge's \`wait\` verdict and planner waits are not reachable in production (owner decision 2).\*\* WorkJudge.wait_probe needs a model rubric, and the shipped runtime passes no rubric, model planner or graders. Wiring one needs the owner's choice of local model and provider under H513 (auxiliary_request_scope, strict-local, security.data_training_ack) plus opt-in settings forced off in safe mode. Before any model planner, the rest of the intake arguments must be clamped: since H464c the task's kind is scope-checked as well as Action.kind, but the task's agent, title and payload still reach govern_enqueue unchanged. - \*\*No graders are wired in production.\*\* After the hub park clears, a run idles until its budget ends. Deterministic verifier and judge adapters belong to the company-mode grading work; they must reuse an existing verdict, because _grade has no try/except and a judge that raises after the verifier recorded leaves \`verdict_already_recorded\` / \`tick_failed\` on every later sweep. Deferred: - Wake-on-fire: a parked run resumes at the next scheduled sweep. - A \`job:\<id\>\` trigger. - A process output-pattern trigger (needs the process producer). - An owner-set wait like Hermes's \`/goal wait\` (owner decision 3): a clock or own-task wait only, never an owner-typed pid. - Waiting between checklist steps: the checklist still advances when a step's task is approved, not when it finishes. - A judge-sourced wait reserves no grading margin (only the hub's park does since H464c), so a judge wait that runs to the end of the budget still leaves the run ungraded. It is reachable only with a judge wait probe (owner decision 2). - The grading margin bounds one run's way back to a sweep, not a queue: with several runs due at once (max_concurrent 1), a parked run can lose the sweep's one slot after its cap. - A blocked run whose budget runs out is not settled (H464d settles only runs the sweep skips as budget_spent; a blocked one is skipped as blocked and its resume is refused once the owner answers), so it stays blocked on the record. - The HUD's live wait credit for an open ask runs to now; if the owner then edits the ask, the durable credit falls back to the last sweep that saw it open (at most one sweep's worth). - An owner stop of a working run is never closed out: request_stop moves it to \`stopping\` and settles only a second stop (agents/core/autonomy/work_runs.py:1382-1384), while ScheduleRuntime.due skips a \`stopping\` run (agents/core/autonomy/schedule_runtime.py:227-228), so the supervisor that would settle it is never ticked and the run reads \`stopping\` until the owner stops it again (reproduced on 03643762). Needs network: re-checking Hermes's goal-wait behaviour at the pinned commit 59b2aeef (hermes_cli/goals.py was not among the inspected upstream sources). Review residuals after round 3 (latent or bounded): a dropped grading sweep is not absorbed by the margin; a transient queue read during the per-sweep wait observation cuts approval-wait credit; a hold open when a run stops is never ended; an expired ask would be re-asked; the upgrade note's 're-approve' advice needs a new card; four test gaps (docs/handoff/2026-09-28-handover/h464-round3-brief.md). ## security

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h481"></a>H481 | Refuse a catastrophic shell command no matter who approved it (hardline floor + risky-pattern tier + de-obfuscation) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The middle tier does not exist.\*\* The row asks for three stacked layers; this is the first, with its matching repaired (and repaired again in PR #1177). There is still no ~40-rule severity/reversibility-tagged risky-pattern catalogue feeding the approval card's description, so \`rm -rf ~/projects\` is neither hardline nor pattern-classified — re-checked 2026-09-19 on 494a13b2: \`hardline_match("rm -rf ~/projects")\` and \`hardline_match(\['rm','-rf','/home/x/projects'\])\` both return None, and the module carries no severity/reversibility/catalogue vocabulary at all — and the owner's card still says "run this argv" rather than "recursive delete", which the row's rationale names as the point. No user-editable \`approvals.deny\` fnmatch list at hardline level (no \`fnmatch\`/\`deny\` in the module). No parser limits that themselves hardline-block and save an oversized payload to a file for manual review (128k chars / 4096 separator-free / 25k segments); \`MAX_ARG_CHARS = 4000\` is argv validation, not a refusal. \*\*Variants Hermes produces and this still does not:\*\* NFKC normalisation, \`$HOME\`/\`$HERMES_HOME\` folding, grep-pattern span masking, and \`python -c\` / \`perl -e\` / \`node -e\` payload extraction — the last left alone on purpose, since \`jobs_scripts.snapshot_script\` legitimately builds \`\[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', source\]\`. \`$()\`/backtick substitution is now extracted in ONE place only: #1177's \`_substituted\` narrows a heredoc BODY line to its substitutions, so \`cat \<\<EOF\` / \`$(rm -rf /)\` / \`EOF\` refuses; an ordinary line is still not expanded, so \`echo $(rm -rf /)\` and a backtick \`rm -rf /\` inside an assignment return None (a \`$(mkfs.ext4 …)\` is caught only through \`_CMD\`'s \`(\` anchor). The row's own examples \`$(printf mkfs).ext4 /dev/sda\` and \`echo bWtmcw==\|base64 -d\|sh\` still return None. \*\*Gaps this module knows it still has, listed honestly rather than as a complete set:\*\* nesting deeper than \`_MAX_SHELL_DEPTH\` (depth-3 \`sh -c "sh -c 'sh -c …'"\` returns None, pinned by test); a quote at a word BOUNDARY (\`'mkfs.ext4' /dev/sda\`) rather than inside a word; \`powershell -EncodedCommand\` base64; a \`cmd /c\` payload split across several argv items; an obfuscated shell HEAD in argv form (\`\['s""h','-c',…\]\`), because the de-obfuscation pass runs after payload extraction and its output is not fed back through it; and, since #1177, two in the false-refusal direction — a \`case\` label with a leading \`(\` (\`(shutdown) echo bye ;;\`) is still read as a command, and a \`case\` nested inside another is not modelled: its inner first label IS stripped (\`case $1 in\\na) case $2 in\\nreboot) echo r ;;\\nesac ;;\\nesac\` went power_cycle→None), so the docstring's 'not handled' is about the region boundary, not a guaranteed refusal. \*\*Refusals #1177 cleared in the dangerous direction — closed by this slice (H481, 2026-09-22).\*\* The \`case\`-label strip fires only inside a real command-position \`case WORD in … esac\` region now, so \`(echo case in; reboot); echo esac\` and the \`$IFS\`-spliced \`(echo case in; mkfs.ext4$IFS/dev/sda); echo esac\` / \`wipefs$IFS-a$IFS/dev/sda\` refuse again; and \`_heredoc_feeds_a_shell\` now also sees a body a shell consumes AFTER the redirection through a pipe (\`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| bash\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| grep … \| sh\`) or via a file a shell later runs (\`cat \<\<'EOF' \>x.sh; sh x.sh\`), so those refuse — and, after the review round, so do \`(sh x.sh)\`, \`{ sh x.sh; }\`, \`if true; then sh x.sh; fi\`, \`cat x.sh \| sh\`, \`sh -c '. x.sh'\`, \`. x.sh\`, \`source x.sh\`, \`tee x.sh \<\<EOF; sh x.sh\`, a quoted \`\> "x.sh"\` target, \`sh /dev/stdin \<\<EOF\`, \`bash -c sh \<\<EOF\` and \`cat \<\<EOF \| eval "$(cat)"\`, each verified to run the body. Pinned in tests/test_local_transport.py, reproduced against /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash first, and screened by a 671,995-case differential fuzz vs origin/main whose every difference is explained by the two changed mechanisms (numbers in the summary; the earlier \`refusals_cleared = 0\` claim is withdrawn). Still open in this direction, listed in \`_detection_variants\` and pinned as misses: a heredoc body written to a file run by a bare path (\`… \>x.sh; ./x.sh\`), by a variable-carried name (\`for f in x.sh; do sh $f; done\`) or by the environment (\`BASH_ENV=x.sh bash -c :\`); a heredoc opened inside a command substitution (\`eval "$(cat \<\<EOF …)"\`, read as quoted text); \`xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'\` (each line becomes the command line); and a \`;\` inside a \`( )\` group of a pipe stage (\`cat \<\<EOF \| (cd /tmp; sh)\`). \*\*Two pre-existing false positives are unchanged\*\* (\`git commit -m "fix;mkfs.ext4 handling"\` → mkfs; \`grep -r "(shutdown" docs/\` → power_cycle), and \`_PATH_PREFIX\` added a third class no approval can lift: a path to a script whose basename starts with a hardline name — \`./mkfs.sh\` (mkfs), \`./reboot.sh\`, \`./scripts/shutdown.sh --dry-run\`, \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` (power_cycle) — all None on 1c5054ea, all refused on 494a13b2 (bare \`reboot.sh\` already refused before; the prefix extends it to every path spelling). This slice left that (c) class unclosed on purpose: any name- or path-only rule that cleared \`./mkfs.sh\` or \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` would also clear a TRUE refusal — \`./mkfs.sh\` is structurally \`mkfs.\<suffix\>\` exactly like the real \`/sbin/mkfs.ext4\`, and \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` is \`\<path\>/\<hardline-name\>\` exactly like a copied \`/tmp/mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` — so it needs approval-context (is the target a workspace script or a real system binary) the pure static floor does not have. \*\*And a measured cost, re-measured after #1177 removed the breadth cap\*\* (\`_MAX_SHELL_EXPANSIONS\` and the \`MAX_ARG_CHARS\` re-expansion guard are gone; every level-1 payload is unwrapped again): on a maximal 64x4000-character argv the pass is roughly unchanged (79.6 ms on the pre-#1177 module vs 82.3 ms after, same machine, best of 5), but a command made of 2,000 nested \`sh -c "sh -c 'echo ok'"\` segments (57k chars) goes from 196 ms to 380 ms — synchronous, on the event loop, before every exec. The docstring's linear-in-input argument holds (each level's payloads are disjoint substrings); an independent re-run on another machine saw 79.0→80.6 ms and 125→151 ms, so the constant grows for nested input but the factor is machine-dependent and no test pins either number. This slice's first pass claimed no measurable cost on that maximal 64x4000-character argv (78.7 → 78.4 ms); the review showed the claim held only for input with no \`case\` word and never exercised the redirect-target branch, which was quadratic. Re-measured after the review round (best of 3, same machine, origin/main → this branch): the maximal 64x4000 argv 62.0 → 58.6 ms; the same argv filled with case/in prose 18,207 → 105 ms (main's own \`\\bcase\\b.\*?\\besac\\b\` was super-linear there too); 4000 chars of \`case x in\` 21.6 → 6.5 ms; eight 4000-char argv items of prose 316 → 14 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\` blocks (12 KB) 39.8 → 64.9 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<E \>x\` blocks in 4000 chars 25 → 39 ms (2,471 ms on the first pass); 1,600 such blocks (33.6 KB) 132 → 201 ms (97.5 s on the first pass); 2,000 nested \`sh -c\` segments 146 → 148 ms; 300 real \`case\` statements 57 → 60 ms. The heredoc-with-target shapes cost about 1.5x main, linear, because the statement lines after a heredoc are tokenised once and shared; nothing else pays. Six test items pin wall-clock bounds (0.25–2 s) two orders of magnitude above these; no test pins a ratio. \*\*Adversarial review of the original slice found four more defects, all fixed and each reproduced first\*\* against real /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash: (1) \`_shell_c_payloads\` demanded the word after the wrappers BE the shell, so \`env FOO=1 sh -c 'mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda'\` — and \`sudo -u root sh -c …\`, \`nice -n 10 sh -c …\` — skipped the whole \`sh -c\` screening while deleting \`FOO=1 \` made the same string \`hardline_denied:mkfs\`; \`_CMD\` had been widened for that assignment prefix and the unwrapper had not. (2) \`_unquoted_lines\` read a backslash-newline as a statement separator when it is a line CONTINUATION, so \`mk\\\` + newline + \`fs.ext4 /dev/sda\`, which all three shells splice into \`mkfs.ext4\`, was seen as two harmless fragments. (3) every heredoc body line became a command position, so \`cat \> x.yaml \<\<'EOF'\` / \`shutdown: graceful\` / \`EOF\` was refused as \`power_cycle\`; body lines were joined as data in the flat text. \*\*#1177's second review showed that fix was incomplete in three ways, all closed there:\*\* \`segments\` still tokenised every body line, so \`cat \> README.md \<\<'EOF'\` / \`rm -rf / will destroy the box\` / \`EOF\` stayed a \`recursive_root_removal\` refusal; the rule had no exception for a heredoc a SHELL consumes, so \`bash \<\<EOF\` / \`mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` / \`EOF\` returned None (a bypass the heredoc work itself opened); and any \`\<\<\` — inside quotes, inside \`$(( ))\`, a \`\<\<\<\` here-string, or with a delimiter that never returns — opened a heredoc and switched the newline screening off for every following line. (4) \`{\` anchored bare, so \`jq '{reboot: .needs_reboot}'\` was a \`power_cycle\` refusal; POSIX requires whitespace after \`{\` for a brace group, and \`{ mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda; }\` is still refused. Each fix is red-proofed on its own. [d1](../agents/core/environments/terminal_contract.py) [d2](../tests/test_local_transport.py) |

| <a id="h495"></a>H495 | Never let a credential reach a log, a transcript, an approval card or an export | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | This is one of the row's seven paths. Hermes applies \`redact.py\` to logs, verbose output, tool output, approval prompts, gateway messages, transcripts and memory; this slice covers LOGS. Still owed, and the rationale calls them the part that matters most: the hash-chained audit DB (a \`cat .env\` from \`terminal_run\` still writes a live credential into an append-only log that by design cannot be edited), the decision-inbox approval card the owner reads on a phone, task payloads, terminal output, and the observability exports — which Hermes scrubs unconditionally with no knob, returning \`\[redaction-unavailable\]\` rather than raw text. Also absent: the second mask mode (a syntactically INVALID \`«redacted:ghp_…»\` sentinel for file reads, so an agent cannot write a masked-but-plausible key back and silently kill the credential), \`code_file=True\` sparing source constants, \`_mask_control_split_tokens()\` for tokens smuggled across ANSI/zero-width characters, and the shared \`.env\` basename list. The two silent-degradation paths named at review are now CLOSED, after the SAST gate forced the question: a handler whose \`addFilter\` fails is counted and published through \`uncovered_handler_count()\` and a warning naming how many handlers are writing unredacted, and a \`loggerDict\` walk that raises warns that coverage fell back to root-only. The one silent swallow left is the filter's own last-ditch blank (\`# nosec B110\`), where a \`logging.Filter\` that raises would take down the caller's log call and the scanner has already failed. \`install_log_redaction()\` remains exported although the \`everywhere\` variant is what production uses. \*\*A later slice's full-suite run surfaced one more thing about this control, recorded here rather than fixed:\*\* the scanner's \`high_entropy_secret\` pattern matches an ordinary filesystem path. Under \`-n auto --dist loadfile\` a warning naming a pytest tmp file arrived as \`truncated: \[REDACTED:high_entropy_secret\].md\`, which is what a real operator would see when a log line names a file with a long random-looking segment — a debuggability cost the module docstring already warns about in general terms, now with a concrete case. Separately, \`setup_logging()\` installs the filter on every handler of every logger and nothing took it back off, so every later test on the same xdist worker ran under a live redactor. The installer is PRODUCTION code, not the tests: \`agents/run.py\` calls \`setup_logging()\` at module level, so importing it installs the filter during pytest COLLECTION — before any fixture runs — and eight test files import it; \`agents/web.py\` calls it from the FastAPI lifespan, so any test that drives the app installs it again; five test files call it directly on top of that. There is no list of leaking files to fix. The cleanup therefore lives in \`tests/conftest.py\` as an autouse fixture that strips unconditionally BEFORE each test as well as after — a snapshot-and-restore fixture, which is what the two neighbouring isolation fixtures do and what this was first written as, treats a collection-time install as pre-existing and preserves it for the life of the worker. That version went green locally and RED in CI on exactly the test it was meant to fix. The first attempt at it matched by \`isinstance\` and silently did nothing: \`agents/\` is on \`sys.path\`, so \`core.security.log_redaction\` and \`agents.core.security.log_redaction\` are two module objects over this one file with two distinct \`SecretRedactionFilter\` classes, and the root handler was carrying the other one. The match is by class name now, and four paired tests pin both halves — including the alternate import path, which is the case the first attempt missed. | <a id="h502"></a>H502 | Refuse an MCP server entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume, and never hand one the owner's keys | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The documented per-server \`env\` migration path does not exist as a reachable surface.\*\* \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field (\`routers/mcp.py:131-143\`, no \`model_config\`), \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` drops the key (pydantic's default \`extra="ignore"\`), and neither \`to_config\` (\`client.py:798-812\`) nor \`load_from_config\` (\`:814-849\`) writes or reads one — the module docstring says as much at \`client.py:41-45\`. The only remedy an owner can actually reach is the \*\*host-wide\*\* \`JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV\` (\`:92-98\`, "Host-wide, not per-server"), which is a strictly weaker grant than the per-server one the docs describe: an owner whose forge server needs \`GITHUB_TOKEN\` must allow it for every stdio server on the host. Whether to ship on that or build the per-server surface first is a product decision, written up as \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` §P28.3 with both options. \*\*Related and also owner-level:\*\* \`XAUTHORITY\` and \`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS\` are both on \`STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST\` (\`client.py:169\`), and today one is listed-but-silently-dropped (\`_SECRET_LIKE\` at \`:174-176\` matches the \`AUTH\` in \`XAUTHORITY\`) while the other is listed-and-handed-over — they should be consistent in one direction or the other. \*\*And the honest ceiling, stated wherever the guarantee is:\*\* this is \*\*defence in depth, not a security boundary\*\*, and since #1179 the module states that ceiling by TWO routes rather than one (\`client.py:17-36\`). The child is same-UID with no namespace, so a server that can run a subprocess reads the withheld variables straight out of \`/proc/\<ppid\>/environ\`; and the baseline narrows the \*\*filesystem\*\* not at all — \`cwd\` defaults to the hub's own working directory, so \`open(".env")\` hands back exactly the keys that were withheld, which \`cwd_secret_files()\` now says out loud at connect (WARNING, filenames only, \`client.py:565-571\`) rather than leaving the owner to infer a protection from the withheld count alone. What the baseline buys is that a \*careless\* or \*curious\* server does not get them by accident, and that an owner's \`JARVIS_\*\` settings are not ambient in a third party's process. It does not stop an adversary, and the row says so rather than implying otherwise; closing the filesystem route as well as the \`/proc\` one needs real process isolation (separate UID, namespace or container — \`client.py:33-36\`), which is not shipped. \*\*Unaddressed from the row:\*\* there is no refusal of an MCP entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume — no command allow-listing, no signature or provenance check on the spawned binary (the docstring itself calls the screener "still-unshipped", \`client.py:34\`), and \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` still stores \`req.command\` verbatim after checking only transport, trust tier, tool-filter shape and bounds, and duplicate name (\`routers/mcp.py:174-227\`, \`:184\`); the only shape check is \`_command_argv()\`'s shell-metacharacter/NUL refusal at connect (\`client.py:519-529\`), which is not a screener. #1179 changed none of this — it explicitly chose disclosure over refusal. This slice hardens what the child \*receives\*; the row also asks who the child is allowed to \*be\*. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py) |
| <a id="h503"></a>H503 | Unpack an archive from another machine without letting it write outside the destination | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | archive_safe is the only module in Nerva that reads an archive. Backup verify and restore unpack through extract_tar_path (agents/core/archive_safe.py:573; agents/core/backup.py:598, agents/core/backup.py:667). Marketplace install_from_zip unpacks through extract_zip_bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:637; agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:816). The old backup._safe_extract and the marketplace _safe_targets+extractall pass are gone. An AST scan (tests/test_archive_safe.py:702, with probes at tests/test_archive_safe.py:688) fails when any other product module imports tarfile or zipfile outside a write-only allowlist, opens an archive for reading or calls an extract-family method. normalize_member (agents/core/archive_safe.py:122) folds backslash to '/' first. It then refuses empty, NUL, POSIX-absolute, Windows-absolute, UNC and drive-lettered names, and any '..' or empty part. Only regular files and directories are written. Symlink, hardlink, device, fifo and sparse tar members raise (agents/core/archive_safe.py:504), and so do symlink, device, fifo, socket and encrypted zip entries (agents/core/archive_safe.py:596). _Planner (agents/core/archive_safe.py:182) validates every member before the first write: name, type, in-archive conflicts and duplicates, member count and declared bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:257). It also checks the destination's existing content (agents/core/archive_safe.py:286): a link on the way to a member is refused, and so is a directory where a file goes (tests/test_archive_safe.py:365, tests/test_backup.py:471). The pre-scan reads the stream to its end (agents/core/archive_safe.py:522), so a bad gzip CRC, trailing data or a corrupt header part-way through is a refusal, not a shortened tree (tests/test_archive_safe.py:482, tests/test_archive_safe.py:517). Bytes are counted again while streaming (agents/core/archive_safe.py:389). The byte budget is clamped to free space plus the blocks of the single-link files the archive replaces (agents/core/archive_safe.py:314), so an in-place --force restore fits (tests/test_backup.py:443). PAX and GNU long-name header payloads are capped before tarfile reads them, 1 MiB each and 256 MiB in total (agents/core/archive_safe.py:461, agents/core/archive_safe.py:483; tests/test_backup.py:515). A link or hardlinked name at a member's path is unlinked and the file is created O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW, never written through (agents/core/archive_safe.py:373; tests/test_archive_safe.py:323, tests/test_archive_safe.py:349). Backups take their caps from JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_MEMBERS and JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_BYTES (agents/core/backup.py:86); skill packages use SKILL_PACKAGE_LIMITS (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:42). On the producing side, create_backup writes both the encrypted and the plaintext branch through atomic_write, a sibling temp file that is fsynced and then os.replace'd (agents/core/archive_safe.py:654; agents/core/backup.py:415, agents/core/backup.py:420). Archives are GNU-format tar (agents/core/backup.py:474). The export walk skips symlinks (agents/core/archive_safe.py:715; agents/core/backup.py:401). publish_skill uses it on the staged copy it signs (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:392); the copy itself is built from the bytes of one source snapshot, which refuses a linked folder, a link or non-regular member and a file that changed while read (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:375), which has its own test (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:219). Every other name, hardlinked ones included, is archived as its own regular member (agents/core/backup.py:434, agents/core/backup.py:458; tests/test_backup.py:388). verify and restore compare the manifest's file_count with what the archive held (agents/core/backup.py:625; tests/test_backup.py:430). install_from_zip stages inside skills_dir, runs the signature gate there, and only then renames the tree into place (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:854, agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:885). A non-zip upload is a 400 refusal (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:194). Staging left by a killed install is swept once it is an hour old (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:773; tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:203). Restore stays an operator CLI action. It prints 'partial restore' when a failure came after the first write (agents/core/backup.py:716) and 'nothing restored' only for a pre-scan refusal (agents/core/backup.py:722). tests/test_backup.py:606 pins that no product module, script or subprocess caller reaches it. The pre-scan also refuses a component longer than the destination's NAME_MAX or a path past PATH_MAX, which the existing-path walk cannot see under a new directory (agents/core/archive_safe.py:251; tests/test_archive_safe.py:726), and a write the destination refuses mid-restore (permissions, a full disk) is a named ArchiveRejected marked partial, never a traceback (agents/core/archive_safe.py:445; tests/test_archive_safe.py:738). A restore leaves the archive's manifest in the target; create_backup never archives a top-level backup_manifest.json from the data root (agents/core/backup.py:482), so a backup of a restored root carries one manifest and verifies, restores and serves the forget purge's verified pre-snapshot (tests/test_backup.py:636, tests/test_backup.py:675). A backup of a restored root made before this change carries that name twice; verify and restore accept exactly that one repeat, last wins as tarfile resolved it, and count the dropped copy against the manifest's file_count (agents/core/backup.py:599, agents/core/backup.py:642; tests/test_backup.py:651). The repeat is bounded: the dropped copy still counts against the member and byte caps, and a third copy or any other duplicate is refused (agents/core/archive_safe.py:220, agents/core/archive_safe.py:223; tests/test_archive_safe.py:768, tests/test_archive_safe.py:775, tests/test_archive_safe.py:790). Known limit: backups made on main from a data root holding a symlink or hardlink fail verify and restore by design. | <a id="h510"></a>H510 | Stop a web page or a rebound DNS name from driving the local API | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The Host guard exempts _PROBE_PATHS (agents/web.py:767, applied at :824-825), so /healthz, /readyz and /metrics answer a rebound name: live-verified, \`Host: evil.test\` returns 200 on /healthz and /metrics and reaches the route on /readyz, while every real API route returns 400. Because DNS rebinding makes the browser treat those responses as same-origin, an attacker page still reads the full Prometheus body (route templates, request counters, latency buckets) and the /readyz body naming the configured LLM backend and the agent/channel counts. The code names this residual itself at agents/web.py:800-802 ("a rebound page can therefore still read the golden-signal counters"). Close it by narrowing the exemption rather than removing it — the operability reason for it is sound. Suggested shape: keep /healthz and /readyz exempt but strip /readyz's detail to a bare ready/not-ready when the Host is not accepted, and require an accepted Host for /metrics unless the request is unambiguously non-browser (no Origin and no Sec-Fetch-Site, or an explicit JARVIS_METRICS_ALLOW_ANY_HOST opt-in for a monitor that scrapes by an unlisted name). Needs a test alongside test_healthz_from_any_host_is_exempt asserting that a refused Host cannot read the counter body. [d1](../agents/core/host_policy.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/proxy_trust.py) |

| <a id="h512"></a>H512 | See who authenticated, who failed, and when | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Add auth event types: admin/user auth success, auth failure with reason (missing, invalid, network-disabled), token issued, token rotated, token revoked. Emit them from _admin_guard and _user_guard: failures always; successes deduplicated per token hash and client so the chain is not flooded. Also emit from TokenStore issue/rotate/revoke_all, which covers the CLI. Each event records scope, client host, reason and a token-hash prefix. A fixed redacted-field set (token, x-admin-token, x-user-token, authorization, cookie, code, state, ticket) is dropped before serialisation. Writes are best-effort: an audit failure must never change the allow/401/403 decision. The rotation route should switch to the new type. Tests must cover all of this. | <a id="h518"></a>H518 | Browse, search and export everything the assistant has generated | keep | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The unified gallery now scans retained generated and attachment metadata through bounded keyset pages. Search and kind filters apply during each scan, including MIME matches; zero-match pages retain continuation to older records. Cursor fields, ordering and filter/source binding are checked before catalog or header I/O. Each request inspects at most 200 candidate headers and retains the existing authenticated path/byte validation. The HUD resets and cancels stale searches, reports retained versus loaded/scanned counts separately, and exports an explicit selection of at most 200 files/128 MiB. Independent review and desktop/responsive-browser tests cover older-only matches, empty-page continuation, deletion boundaries, source flags, malformed cursors and exact export selection. Shared attachment previews now abort on card unmount and reject late fetch/body continuations; owned object URLs are revoked even before a pending React state update commits. Reassessment against the frozen keep/superior row confirms the bounded retained-catalog browse/search/export contract. Retention and unavailable-byte handling match the original scope; deleted/evicted history restoration, native controls and unlimited ZIPs are not acceptance requirements. Timestamp/key traversal excludes newer appends without promising exact membership against backdated inserts. The current local generator catalogs produced assets; legacy cloud proposals do not execute a cloud image backend. Future approved cloud completion must persist catalog output as part of H515, not be inferred from generic text-task completion. No live-provider or universal producer-provenance proof is claimed. The implemented cloud-image producer now writes canonical validated output and an idempotent opt-in catalog record before successful completion (if only that catalog write fails after the image and its completion record are durable, the task still succeeds with warning catalog_record_failed and a later recover, e.g. the generation-task status read, adds the row idempotently); independent signed-worker/mock-provider/blob/ZIP checks cover the previously conditional writeback obligation. The legacy generic text task is not treated as an image producer. Current-source reassessment 2026-09-27: reviewed the changed shared HUD, multimodal router, local image runtime/writer, Telegram decision routing and protected settings against this frozen contract. Gallery pagination/search/selection/export, opaque authenticated delivery and scheduled-media recipient/content/deadline/uncertain-state controls remain intact. New registered local producer reaches the opt-in gallery and authenticated readback with its correct backend label. At that reassessment the full offline suites passed 19,363 backend tests (35 skipped) and 1,829 frontend tests. Limits remain retained opt-in metadata, bounded bytes/exports, signature-level non-PNG validation and no live provider/native-codec guarantee. See the H517 local-provider review for the specific shared-path analysis. Shared-registry compatibility review: image execution now calls the selected concrete adapter while preserving ComfyUI option validation, exact config/approval checks and backend factory semantics. Image/edit/workflow regressions pass; no extra edit/upscale capability is claimed. ComfyUI shared-source review (2026-10-01): the changed backend now forwards the existing runtime guard and rechecks authority after download and immediately before atomic publication. Reviewed the adapter delta and the new backend/worker regressions: revoked results are withheld without a PNG or catalog success, while machinery failures preserve their own reason. The unchanged gallery finalization/readback/export flow and image option vocabulary remain in force. This fixes the handover publication defect; it adds no provider inventory, VRAM orchestration or live-service acceptance. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h526"></a>H526 | Make a spoken reply sound like speech rather than read-out markdown | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A spoken reply now sounds like speech rather than read-out markdown, as Hermes requires, at every text-to-speech entry point. agents/core/voice/speech_text.py is the one normaliser (for_speech, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:152). It drops \<think\>/\<thinking\>/\<reasoning\> blocks, an unclosed one to the end, and everything before a closing tag that never opened. It drops fenced code (\`\`\` or ~~~, an unclosed fence to the end) and HTML tags. \[text\](url) reads as its text, !\[alt\](url) as its alt, and a bare or \<autolinked\> URL as "link"; the URL no longer eats a closing parenthesis or the sentence's full stop, so "doc (https://…)." is "doc (link).". Headings, list items (-, \*, +, •, 1., 1), task boxes), block quotes and table rows lose their markers and end with a full stop (_line, :124). Table cells read with commas; separator rows and horizontal rules are dropped. Bold, italic, strikethrough and inline-code markers go and their words stay (_inline, :113), with word guards so 2\*3\*4 and my_var_name survive. Symbols are said in the reply's language (_symbols, :104, WORDS, :79): & as and/și, % as percent/la sută, -\> → =\> as to/spre, and °, °C, °F as degrees/grade (Celsius, Fahrenheit). The language comes from the request, else a ro-RO-… voice, else the engine default (speech_lang, :95). Emoji go: pictographs, flags, keycaps, skin tones, ZWJ sequences, variation selectors and tag flags (_EMOJI, :61). Fish \[emotion\] tags are kept intact. Text with no letter or digit left is "" (_SAYABLE, :76), and the result is a fixed point. Wiring: TTSEngine.speak normalises before any backend, Fish included, and synthesises nothing when nothing is left (agents/core/voice/tts.py:212-220). An emotion-tags-only reply is also nothing for the other backends (:263). speak_stream normalises the whole reply before splitting sentences (:315), so a code or reasoning block spanning sentences is dropped whole. Through TTSEngine.speak this covers the wake-word VoicePipeline, VoiceChannel.send and chat voice notes: speakable() is for_speech plus its cap, and SpokenReply passes the chat's language (agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:69). POST /tts answers 204 with X-Nerva-Speech: nothing_to_say instead of synthesising (agents/core/routers/voice.py:76), and /tts/stream frames carry the spoken sentences. The HUD twin is frontend/src/speech-text.ts (speechText, frontend/src/speech-text.ts:105). Both implementations are pinned by the same cases (frontend/src/test/speech-text-cases.json, 47 cases, read by both suites). SpeechStreamFilter (:129) drops a fenced block or reasoning block whose markers arrive split across token deltas, holding back a tail that could still become a marker (including a \<reasoning …\> tag split inside its attributes). An unclosed block at the end is never spoken. frontend/src/voice.ts uses it on the live sentence stream (:281, :332, :343). It normalises the whole reply for /tts/stream, /tts and the browser's own speechSynthesis voice (:204), and every streamed sentence (:302). A 204 is silence, never an empty clip or the fallback voice (:244, :314). Mobile sends the reply to /tts, so the hub normalises it; ttsFetchBase64 reads 204 as '' and speak() finishes at once (mobile/src/api/client.ts:2402, mobile/src/audio/tts.ts:23). Tests: tests/test_h526_speech_text.py (Python, every shared case plus the fixed-point property), frontend/src/test/speech-text.test.ts and voice-speech-text.test.tsx (a live push-to-talk turn with a fence split across deltas), and mobile/src/api/__tests__/tts.test.ts. 126 mutants: 124 caught after nine cases were added; the other two were redundant code, removed. Named speech compatibility review: normalization still precedes synthesis, persona consent and tags retain existing behavior, and HTTP nothing-to-say/stream paths are unchanged. Shared speech-text and voice regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speech_text.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/tts.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py) |

| <a id="h528"></a>H528 | Deliver audio as a native voice message on a chat platform | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h529"></a>H529 | Transcribe a voice message or dictated speech | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un singur backend. Hermes are opt (faster-whisper local, \`local_command\`, Groq, OpenAI, Mistral Voxtral cu diarizare și timestamp-uri pe cuvânt, xAI, ElevenLabs Scribe, DeepInfra) plus provideri pe comandă de shell, un ABC \`TranscriptionProvider\`, selecție explicită strictă cu auto-detect doar la o instalare neconfigurată vreodată, un plafon pe lungimea promptului Whisper, trunchierea liniștii înainte de upload ca să scadă octeții trimiși, un picker și \`POST /api/audio/transcribe\`. Nimic din astea nu e livrat. Nu din lene: clauza de guvernanță a rândului spune că orice STT în cloud trece prin \`plugin.egress\` și trimite vorbirea înregistrată a owner-ului în afara cutiei — e aceeași clasă de dezvăluire ca TTS-ul în cloud și trebuie clasificat, nu pus ca implicit de config. Cache-ul motorului stă tot în \`routers/voice.py\`, care e casa greșită pentru el; canalul îl importă în loc să-l dubleze, iar mutarea lui e o schimbare separată. NEVERIFICAT pe servicii reale: niciun model Whisper încărcat cu adevărat. [d1](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/inbound_voice.py) |
| <a id="h530"></a>H530 | Not turn a silent recording into a message | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whisper nu tace când nu aude nimic — inventează. Pe liniște întoarce o propoziție fluentă din datele de antrenament: generice de subtitrare („Subtitles by the Amara.org community", „Subtitrarea: ..."), formule de încheiere („Thanks for watching!", „Mulțumesc pentru vizionare!"). Pentru o casetă de dictare e o enervare; aici e un control de siguranță, exact cum spune clauza de guvernanță a rândului: un mesaj vocal devine o tură \*inbound\*, o tură inbound poate cere lucruri, deci un transcript halucinat e o instrucțiune pe care n-a autorizat-o nimeni, intrată într-un sistem guvernat. \`hallucination.py\` o oprește. Designul e dictat de asimetria erorilor: a pierde un mesaj real e inacceptabil (cineva zice „mulțumesc" și dispare fără răspuns), a lăsa unul să treacă costă o tură ignorabilă. De aici două niveluri, nu o listă: frazele distinctive (\`CANNED\`) cad pe text; cele pe care oamenii chiar le spun (\`AMBIGUOUS\`) cad doar când durata \*decodată\* arată că înregistrarea era prea scurtă ca să le conțină — iar un apelant care nu știe durata le păstrează. Potrivirea e pe transcriptul întreg, niciodată pe substring; regula pentru generice cere marker ȘI vocabular exclusiv de generic, fiindcă prima variantă (doar markerul) mânca „am văzut pe amara.org un film bun". Filtrul rulează în \`STTEngine.transcribe\` și întoarce \`\[silence\]\` — santinela care exista deja și pe care fiecare apelant o tratează ca „nu s-a spus nimic" — deci ruta de dictare din browser o primește gratuit, nu doar calea nouă de pe Telegram. CORECTARE la auditul din 07.09: verdictul „missing" era pe jumătate greșit. A doua cerință a rândului — auto-stop pe liniște (VAD), ca utilizatorul să nu apese o tastă — EXISTĂ de mult în \`frontend/src/voice.ts\`: \`recordUtterance\` eșantionează RMS la 60 ms și oprește după \`SILENCE_MS\`, cu plafon dur și abandon când nimeni nu vorbește. Auditul a dat un \`rg -i 'vad'\` peste \`frontend/src/\` și a raportat „nimic relevant"; linia 111 spune „VAD" în litere. Ce lipsea cu adevărat era dovada: nimic nu o testa, fiindcă harness-ul comun folosește un MediaRecorder care se oprește singur și scurtcircuitează intervalul. \`voice-vad.test.tsx\` folosește unul care NU se oprește niciodată singur — dacă o tură se termină acolo, doar detectorul de liniște a putut-o termina — și acoperă fereastra de liniște, plafonul, abandonul și faptul că vorbirea continuă resetează ceasul. Named speech compatibility review: the Whisper hallucination filter and decoded-duration handling remain on the native transcribe path; named commands preserve the existing exact-sentinel behavior. Hallucination and speech selection regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d3](../frontend/src/voice.ts) |

| <a id="h545"></a>H545 | Let the editor hand the agent MCP servers that exist only for that session | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Every accepted part is missing: (1) an ACP session entry point (session/new, load, resume, fork) that accepts an mcpServers array; this depends on H542. (2) A session-scoped MCP registry layered over the global MCPManager, so that a server registered for one session is invisible to every other session and is torn down when the session ends. (3) Off-loop registration (asyncio.to_thread) with a bounded discovery join (default 1.5 s), so a dead server cannot block session creation. (4) A scheduled late refresh that re-reads the session's tool list after slow discovery finishes. (5) Governance: session-supplied servers pass through the same outbound MCP authorization gate as configured servers (H554, still partial), and their trust tier is capped at no wider than a configured server's. Un filtru malformat îngustează, nu lărgește: un allow care nu e listă devine \`\[\]\` (\`_clean_tool_patterns\`, :124-135). Limitele (64 de pattern-uri / 128 de caractere, :120-121) sunt fail-closed. Un deny care nu poate fi păstrat (peste 64 de pattern-uri, un pattern peste 128 de caractere, sau care nu e listă) venit dintr-un config editat manual prin \`load_from_config\` îngustează serverul la nimic (\`tools_allow = \[\]\`) și loghează un avertisment (:407-418), pentru că un deny tăiat ar face din nou vizibilă unealta pe care o numea. Filtrele fac round-trip prin \`to_config\`/\`load_from_config\` (:808-809, :852-853). Teste (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py, 13/13 verzi): listare și deny prevalează (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:47-57), unealtă filtrată refuzată la apel pe nume (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:60-70), filtru malformat → nimic și round-trip de config (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:73-84), ruta primește filtre și refuză formele malformate (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:98-109), ruta refuză 129 de caractere / 65 de intrări pe ambele câmpuri (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:121-129) și acceptă un filtru exact la limită (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:132-138), un deny imposibil de păstrat ascunde toate uneltele cu avertisment (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:141-147), iar un config editat manual cu deny peste limite se încarcă fail-closed fără să atingă serverul vecin (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:150-159).
