## docs-features

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h575"></a>H575 | Read the product's own per-feature manual (published docs site) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h576"></a>H576 | Read a PDF / Office document / notebook as text from inside a tool call | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Formats stop at .pdf and .docx: no .ipynb, .xlsx, .doc, .ppt, .odt, .rtf or .epub, and the text comes back plain rather than converted to Markdown. There is no size refusal for documents (Hermes refuses over 50 MB): the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned. There is no scanned-page coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text, which the row calls the clever half. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. A missing parser is named (\`parser_missing\`) rather than installed lazily. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../tests/test_file_search.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_read_documents.py) |
| <a id="h577"></a>H577 | Give the current conversation one recurring instruction (session heartbeat) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h578"></a>H578 | Re-run a prompt on a cadence inside the session, self-paced, with stop conditions (/loop) | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h579"></a>H579 | Pull a file or slice of a file into a message inline (@ context references) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner's own chat attaches @file:path, @file:path#L10-40 and @file:"a b.md" for the turn. agents/core/context_refs.py holds the rules. expand (agents/core/context_refs.py:209) finds a reference that starts a word or follows an opening bracket, quote or backtick, and not one inside a word or a path (x@file:, a/@file:, @@file:) (_REF_RE, agents/core/context_refs.py:49); a double-quoted path may hold spaces, #L7 is one line and #L2-L3 a range, and trailing punctuation or a closing quote is not part of it. The routes keep the owner's message as typed: commands, skills, LLM-backend control, the router and memory see only it. The "--- Attached Context ---" section is bound to the turn (bind_attached, agents/core/context_refs.py:251; agents/web.py:1245 on /chat, and inside the stream task at agents/web.py:1312) and appended after the message only in the model prompt (attached_block, agents/core/context_refs.py:266; Orchestrator._build_agent_turn_text, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3331), so the file text is never stored as the owner's words, replayed in a later turn's history or embedded. Hermes keeps the expanded message in the user turn; Nerva shows the attachment to the model for the attaching (tainted) turn only. Each file's text is in a fence longer than any backtick run in it, so no line of it can close the fence (_fence, agents/core/context_refs.py:194), under a header that names the file as typed and the lines actually attached, \`(line 7)\` or \`(lines 2-3)\`, as attached file content, not instructions; injection patterns and a forged Attached Context marker are flagged in that header (\`flagged: …\`, _flags, agents/core/context_refs.py:180), not withheld. Each path is resolved through the same FileScope as file_read (_attach_one, agents/core/context_refs.py:133), so outside the file roots, .. traversal, a symlink escape and a secret-looking path such as .env are refused. A missing file, a directory, a binary file (a NUL in its first 8 KB), a bad line range, a range past the end, an unreadable file, an over-long name, a denied stat and an unknown ~user each become a one-line warning in the same section instead of failing the message. With JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS unset, the file tools' default root \<data home\>/workspace is used; only an unusable JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS makes the section say nothing was attached. The limits are 8 distinct references per message, 64 KB per reference and 128 KB in all, counted in bytes. A cut reference says so, and a repeated one is attached once. A line range skips the lines before it a 64K-character chunk at a time, up to 64M characters into the file (MAX_SCAN_CHARS; past it, the warning "line N is too far into the file"), keeps at most the byte budget and never reads a line whole (_read_lines, agents/core/context_refs.py:105); invalid UTF-8 is read with replacement characters. The expansion runs on /chat and /chat/stream (agents/web.py:1234, agents/web.py:1399), off the event loop, so both the HUD and nerva chat get it. It never runs for a channel message, as Hermes' gateway does not expand either. A turn that attached anything is tainted: handle_input and handle_input_stream call _taint_attached_context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:357, :1966, :2169), which raises the turn's action origin to recall:untrusted. An action planned from attached content therefore escalates GRANT to QUEUE at the kernel, while an inbound turn keeps its more specific origin. complete (agents/core/context_refs.py:272) lists in-scope completions for what follows @file:, with directories ending in / and a path with a space quoted (a name no reference could name is left out). It shows dot files only when asked and never secret paths or links out of the roots, is bounded to 50 entries, and returns nothing for a prefix over 1,024 characters. GET /api/context-refs (agents/core/routers/commands.py:63, user-guarded, read-only) serves it and the reference types, and answers 503 file_roots_unusable when the roots cannot be used. The HUD composer (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:228, :283, frontend/src/context-refs.tsx) offers the reference types while \`@\` or \`@fi\` is typed (Tab takes \`@file:\`), then the in-scope paths; a list is shown only for the text it was fetched for. tests/test_h579_context_refs.py (70 cases) covers the bounds, parsing, whole files, line ranges (single, L-form, past the end), every refusal and warning, a symlink, punctuation, word starts, dedupe, the byte cut, the shared budget, the reference limit, an unreadable file, invalid UTF-8, the roots, an explicit scope and the marker; completion (prefixes, dot files, secrets, roots, a long prefix, a file or missing folder, the bound); both routes, the completion route, the turn taint on both paths, and an inbound turn. The review round added attached text never read as the owner's command (a slash command, a skill, a backend switch) and never stored or carried to a later turn, through the real routes; a file that cannot close its fence; injection and a forged marker flagged; a range in one huge line bounded in memory and a range of many short lines linear; a range too far into a file; quoted paths and their completion; references after brackets and quotes and not inside words; the distinct-reference limit; the header's path and lines; paths the system refuses to look at; and the roots. frontend/src/test/context-refs.test.tsx has 6 cases. The row's 53 mutants predate this code; the review round's 34: 28 caught at once, five after five cases were added, and one equivalent (a kept read stops at the budget either way, since readline(0) answers ""). Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. [d1](../agents/core/context_refs.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h586"></a>H586 | Paste or attach a screenshot into the conversation (vision & image paste) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Both halves of the accepted adaptation are delivered: the HUD composer and the terminal. useComposerImages (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx) takes image paste and drag-drop (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx:51-52) plus a file chooser. It accepts at most eight PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP images of up to 4 MiB each, and the shared InputBar wires it in (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:246). App.submit sends an image draft to runVision (frontend/src/app.tsx:410, frontend/src/app.tsx:385). runVision posts to the user-guarded POST /api/vlm/composer/describe (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:221, registered at agents/web.py:1529). The route checks data-URI signatures and bounds raster decoding (validate_raster, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:53). It binds the request to the reviewed destination/model revision and refuses a non-local VLM unless the owner has acknowledged it (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:251). It then calls VLMBackend.generate_vision_checked (agents/core/llm/vlm.py:354). Since H513 the binding also covers the credential and the data policy, the H378 selection guards refuse a flagged model before any client is built (409), the configuration and the wire are checked again at the request and after cleanup (a change is never an answer: it is a 409 vlm_destination_changed, or a 502 vlm_generation_failed when the changed configuration is no longer a valid vision destination, such as a non-http(s) URL), and the status and the answer carry the data-policy warning, which the composer and the vision reply show (nerva chat --image does not print it). Images reach only the configured VLM, never the text chat model. The taint governance holds through isolation rather than a taint flag. The vision answer is a client-only transcript entry, and ordinary turns post only {message, agent} to /chat/stream (frontend/src/app.tsx:336), so no tool plan can be derived from image content. tests/test_composer_vision.py (33 passed) covers validation, auth, binding, the remote acknowledgement and error shapes, and, since H513, the warning on a pure status, a key rotation that revises the binding, a policy rotated during client construction, a key rotated during cleanup, the H378 selection refusal and a cancelled generation that closes the client. Intake and the vision turn are covered by frontend/src/test/composer-images.test.tsx, frontend/src/test/composer-vision-turn.test.tsx and frontend/e2e/composer-images.spec.ts, (the two vitest files, 12 cases, pass; the Playwright spec was not run here). The terminal: \`nerva chat --image PATH\` (repeatable, up to eight) and \`--clipboard-image\` (agents/cli/nerva.py:353) turn the chat verb into the same vision turn (_vision_turn, agents/cli/nerva.py:2387), dispatched before any /chat body is built (agents/cli/nerva.py:2039). Each file is opened once without blocking, must be a regular file, is read up to 4 MiB and sent by the type its signature says, never its name (_image_mime, agents/cli/nerva.py:2130; _read_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:2140). The clipboard is read by the platform's own reader with a fixed argv, no shell, its output read no further than the bound against a 10 s deadline, in a session of its own whose whole process group is killed at the deadline, past the bound or on an interrupt, so a descendant holding the pipe cannot stretch it (_run_reader, agents/cli/nerva.py:2240): wl-paste on Wayland, xclip on X11, pngpaste or else osascript on macOS, Windows PowerShell from the system directory, else the first powershell.exe or pwsh.exe in an absolute PATH entry that is not the current directory (never shutil.which, which puts the current directory back on Windows), on Windows, and through interop under WSL (the /mnt/c default, else the PATH's powershell.exe) (_clipboard_command, agents/cli/nerva.py:2215; _clipboard_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:2325). The verb reads GET /api/vlm/composer/status, sends that destination and binding with the images, so a model changed in between is refused (409), and a destination off the hub's machine is used only when --remote-vision names it, the terminal's form of the HUD's per-destination acknowledgement (agents/cli/nerva.py:2457). The describe call waits up to 240 s (the hub gives the model 180 s), and a timeout after the status answered is a failed turn, not a missing hub; the reply is checked as the HUD checks it (ok, a string answer of at most 128 KiB); a status without its destination, binding or local sends nothing. The --remote-vision address is compared as the hub names it (scheme and host in any case, the default port, a trailing slash); one with a user name, query or fragment is refused as a look-alike, and --remote-vision without an image is refused. A body the route refuses is answered with its first reason as {error, reason}, never FastAPI's default 422 that echoed the images back (_BoundedValidationRoute, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:22), and the CLI prints it, with the field a validation reason names; hub text is filtered before it reaches the terminal. --agent, --session and --reasoning are refused with images: the turn goes to the vision model only, never to /chat, an agent or a session. -z, --json and --usage-file keep their meaning. tests/test_nerva_chat_image.py (30 cases) covers the route taken, the real route answering the CLI, a model changed in between, each raster kind, the remote acknowledgement, the refusals before any request, the hub's answers and the clipboard readers; its 20 mutants are all caught. The first adversarial review (0 major, 6 minor, 9 nits) found the 30 s timeout reported as no hub, the clipboard buffered whole, the route's refusal without its reason, PowerShell resolved from the current directory, 16 surviving mutants of its own, and macOS/WSL gaps; all are fixed, and tests/test_h586b_image_review.py (50 cases) pins them, its 35 mutants all caught. Test manual: CHT-115, CHT-116. The second review (0 major, 4 minor, 8 nits) found an oversized clipboard image reported as empty, the deadline stretched by a descendant, the Windows fallback still reaching the current directory through shutil.which, and 8 real survivors; all are fixed, tests/test_h586c_image_review.py (23 cases, real processes for the deadline, the group kill, the exit code and the bound) pins them, and its 16 mutants are all caught but one equivalent. Known limits: the receipt's model stays null for an image turn, and the route's own 403 (no acknowledgement) is told apart from an auth 403 by its message, a case the binding already makes unreachable. Composer image recovery review (2026-10-02): enabled recovery is exposed by paired validated empty_retries/retry_notice metadata. HUD renders the notice before remote consent and resets acknowledgment on binding changes; CLI validates and prints the notice before acknowledgment/send. Default shapes and stdout/JSON stay unchanged. Actual CLI and web composer tests, full1847 frontend tests, typecheck, E2E TypeScript compilation and build pass; browser E2E execution was not run. Native composer normalization review (2026-10-02): VLM validates and normalizes compatible replies on both budget-disabled and active governed paths. Useful typed reasoning supplies an answer without an extra call; malformed envelopes remain failures. CLI/HUD entrypoints, acknowledgment and public shapes are unchanged. Parent696 integration includes composer/policy tests; frontend/schema/assets match the prior1847-test verified snapshot. Existing equivalent status preserved. Publication review correction (2026-10-02): agents/core/llm/native_response.py:72 now admits only legacy untyped, reasoning.text and reasoning.summary details. Encrypted, opaque, unknown and malformed typed details are skipped even if they contain string summary/content/text. Seven actual governed image/video regressions failed before the fix; the corrected normalization and empty-recovery union passed all163 cases. Opaque-only video refuses in one call without granting retry authority. All base evidence hashes were current before this two-file correction; unchanged authority, transport, route and UI contracts retain their existing assessment. | <a id="h659"></a>H659 | A retried external request returns the original run instead of starting a second one | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The three external intakes now honour an Idempotency-Key, so a retried request returns the original run instead of starting a second one. agents/core/idempotency.py holds the rules. parse_key (agents/core/idempotency.py:79) accepts 1-255 visible ASCII characters and refuses anything else with HTTP 400 invalid_idempotency_key. check_header (agents/core/idempotency.py:322) runs before the body is read. On POST /api/webhooks/{id} (after the hub's 503 not-initialized check) and on POST /api/a2a/task it also runs before the token, signature or peer is checked; POST /api/actions/request authenticates the caller first (user_guard), then checks the key. IdempotencyStore (agents/core/idempotency.py:105) keeps a SQLite row whose primary key is (scope, key), in the data folder (idempotency.db). reserve (agents/core/idempotency.py:147) runs inside BEGIN IMMEDIATE, so two workers or processes on the same file cannot both win (8 racing threads get exactly one reservation), and a reservation survives a restart. A row holds only the fingerprint (SHA-256 of method, path and body, each length-prefixed; agents/core/idempotency.py:88) and the public reference of what the request started, capped at 2 KB. It never holds the body, a reply or a credential. A retry with the same fingerprint returns that reference with Idempotent-Replayed: true, and nothing runs again. The same key with a different request gets 409 idempotency_key_reused. A retry while the first attempt is still running gets 409 idempotency_in_progress with Retry-After. A first attempt that raised or answered 5xx releases the key, so its retry runs. So does a webhook delivery whose agent turn produced no answer: an error or timeout marker from the hook's own target agent, a backend failure reply, the no-model line, a data-handling refusal or a refused turn, which the orchestrator answers as text instead of raising (is_failed_turn_reply, agents/core/orchestrator.py:110; _attempt_failed, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:583); and a deliver-only delivery whose push failed. The marker must name the hook's target exactly, and the target is not validated. A hook whose target is not a loaded agent id (a typo, "Jarvis", a removed agent) runs a routed turn, where the routed agent's "\[jarvis timeout\]" or "\[jarvis error: …\]" is not recognised. That delivery keeps its key, and a retry replays the failed turn. A turn that answered keeps its key even when its push failed. Each reservation carries a random attempt id, and complete, release and touch require it. A webhook delivery refreshes its row every 60 s while it runs (Claim.kept_alive, agents/core/idempotency.py:288); a row nothing refreshed for 600 s (its process died) is taken over under a new attempt id, so the first attempt can no longer finish or release it. A wall-clock jump larger than the window can still cause a take-over between heartbeats. Rows expire after 24 h, swept one bounded batch of 500 per reservation on a created_at index; a key's own expired row is dropped when that key is reserved. There is no per-scope row cap. The routes call the store through asyncio.to_thread, never on the event loop (a release on the exception path excepted). When the store cannot be written, a keyed request is refused with 503 idempotency_unavailable rather than run without its guarantee. That covers the reservation: an outcome that cannot be recorded afterwards is tried three times, then logged, and the row stays pending until it goes stale, after which a retry runs the request again. durable (agents/core/idempotency.py:129) says whether the store is a file rather than :memory:. The scope is the authenticated caller, and the key is reserved only after authentication and before anything is queued or run, so a duplicate never becomes a second approval item, kernel task or audit row. POST /api/actions/request (agents/core/routers/actions.py:76) reserves in the owner's action scope after the body is validated. A replay returns the queued action, or its id if the queue no longer holds it. POST /api/a2a/task (agents/core/routers/a2a.py:73-74) reserves in the scope a2a:\<peer\> only after the peer's signature verifies. A forged retry is refused with 401 and never reads the peer's receipt, and a refused task releases its key. The stored receipt is id, status and accepted. POST /api/webhooks/{id} (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:552) reserves in the scope webhook:\<id\> after the token or signature, the receiver switch and the enabled check, and before the delivery is counted, run or sent. _run_delivery (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:609) is the previous delivery body, unchanged. _public_outcome (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:603) keeps only ok, target, skipped and error; a replay answers those with the original status code, never the reply, steps or delivery. tests/test_h659_idempotency.py (90 cases) covers the key grammar, the fingerprint, and every store state: TTL, stale take-over, restart, two workers, a thread race, an in-memory store, the reference cap, an unreadable reference, and rollback on a file and in memory. It also covers the begin/claim mapping and all three routes: replay, conflict, no key, a malformed key checked first, an invalid body or unauthenticated caller not burning a key, a failed attempt released, scopes per hook and per peer, a skipped delivery replayed, and a forged A2A retry. The review round added fencing, the heartbeat, the bounded sweep and its index, per-key expiry, the migration of a file from before attempts, the outcome's retries, begin and done off the loop in all three routes, and the failed-turn and failed-push release rules. There are 57 mutants, all killed; the review round's 20 mutants: 19 killed (3 by tests added after a first pass) and 1 equivalent (PURGE_BATCH 500 to 5: the sweep test is written against the constant). Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. | <a id="h666"></a>H666 | The agent's own task list nests subtasks under a parent | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 and fixed after one adversarial review (review-H666: no major; five minors and eight nits, all fixed). Hermes nests the agent's subtasks with one optional parent on each todo item (the id of another item) instead of a nested array, so merge-by-id keeps working, and draws the list through one defensive tree builder. Nerva now does both, for the todo tool's items and for mission plan steps. The tree builder: - agents/core/todo_tree.py tree() (agents/core/todo_tree.py:48) returns (item, depth) pairs in depth-first order, children in list order under their parent, with every item exactly once. It is stdlib only, because the CLI imports it. - Depth is capped at MAX_DEPTH = 4 (agents/core/todo_tree.py:30); a deeper item is drawn at depth 4, under its parent. - An item whose parent is missing, itself, or not an id at all is drawn at depth 0 in its place (agents/core/todo_tree.py:65). ref() accepts only text or whole numbers as ids; a bool, a fractional float, a list or a dict is no id, and a whole-valued float is its number, as JSON's 1.0 is 1 to the HUD (agents/core/todo_tree.py:33). When two items share an id, the first holds it. Anything that is not a list is an empty tree. - Cycle members, and anything under them, are reached from no root, so they are appended flat at depth 0 in list order (agents/core/todo_tree.py:78). A getter that raises counts as no parent. The walk uses an explicit stack, so a 5,000-item chain costs no recursion. - The HUD's twin is frontend/src/todo-tree.ts todoTree() (frontend/src/todo-tree.ts:28), with the same rules. Both suites read one shared file of 47 cases, frontend/src/test/todo-tree-cases.json (hand-written shapes plus seeded random ones over text, number, whole and fractional float, bool, null, list and dict ids and parents), and assert the same drawn order and depths. The review's fuzz had found the twins differing on whole-valued floats; that is fixed and pinned. The todo tool: - An item may carry parent: the id of the item it sits under. It is an allowed item field (agents/core/todo_tool.py:84) and is in the schema as a string of at most MAX_ID (agents/core/todo_tool.py:102); the description tells the model how to nest and un-nest. - _clean_parent (agents/core/todo_tool.py:197) cleans it like an id. Empty or whitespace-only text means no parent, as Hermes clears it. Anything else that is not an id is refused as todo_bad_parent, with a sentence the model can act on, and changes nothing. - A parent need not name an item in the list: a dangling, self or cyclic parent is stored and drawn at the top, never refused and never lost. - On a merge, parent null means not sent (as for content and status), and "" clears it. A changed parent counts as a write, so the plan cap applies. A parent moved by an untrusted turn taints the item, as a status does. Re-sending the same parent changes nothing: no taint, and the plan's labels stay (agents/core/todo_tool.py:400). - model_items answers the parent only when it is set (agents/core/todo_tool.py:227), so a flat list answers exactly as before and plan_bytes counts a parent's bytes. Mission plan steps: - MissionStore.create (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:203) gives every step a nullable parent. _plan_step (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:390) reads each entry. - A title, or any other value, is a top-level step with its own text, as before; a mapping without title or parent is still stringified. - {title, parent?} may sit under an earlier step: parent is that step's index, and only an earlier one. A plan is therefore a forest by construction. - Anything else is refused as bad_plan_step: a self, later, out-of-range, negative, bool, text or float parent, a missing or blank title, or an unknown key. - POST /api/missions (agents/core/routers/missions.py:50) accepts such a plan. A bad step answers 400 with one fixed sentence and code bad_plan_step (agents/core/routers/missions.py:73); no request data reaches the body. - The build-queue plan kept out-of-range and self parents on mission steps and let the tree degrade them. The store refuses them instead: a mission plan is written once, by its creator, so a bad parent there is a caller's mistake worth a 400. The tree still degrades any step the store did not check, such as a row written before this change, which has no parent key. A legacy entry {title, \<other key\>} is now refused too; nothing in the repo sends one. - finish_step budget charging and the completion gates are unchanged. A child step is charged like any step, closing a child does not close its parent, and exhausting the budget still auto-fails the mission. Rendering: - nerva todo draws the tree through todo_tree, indented two spaces a level (agents/cli/nerva.py:1720-1723). - The Decision Inbox's plans in flight (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx) orders items through todoTree and indents each row 14 px a level, with data-depth (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx:78-93). ITEMS_SHOWN counts rows in tree order. - MissionCanvasPanel draws plan steps through todoTree, the tree keyed by each step's idx (frontend/src/panels/mission-canvas.tsx:248). The finish controls name the step's own idx, not its drawn position: the test clicks done in a nested row drawn third and asserts it posts that step's idx. Tests: - tests/test_h666_subtasks.py (39 cases): - the tree: DFS order, a child listed before its parent, the depth cap, dangling, self and cyclic parents, non-id parents, duplicate ids, a getter that raises, and a long chain; - todo parents: storage, merge, clearing, cleaning, refusal, the cap, labels, taint, the schema and the tool answer; - mission step parents in the store and through POST /api/missions, with the refusal cases, budget charging on a child step, and nerva todo indentation. - Vitest: frontend/src/test/todo-tree.test.ts (9, including the shared cases), plus one case each in the plans-in-flight and mission-canvas suites (indentation, dangling or cyclic parents kept in view, and the finish control's idx). - 43 mutants of the new guards were all killed (four survivors of the first run were killed by added cases). One unreachable guard, a placed-twice check in the walk, was removed. The review's 51 mutants left three survivors: two (a boolean read as an id in the TS twin, a finish control addressing its drawn position) are now killed, and the third (the tree keyed by position) is equivalent, since a step's idx is its position. Six mutants of the fix round's guards were all killed. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h670"></a>H670 | The default identity prompt is a behavior contract, and it lives in one file | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The shared identity band exists. agents/_identity/IDENTITY.md is one behaviour contract: the reply-sizing rule (match length to the weight of the ask), earned depth, the named prohibitions (no filler, no restating the request, no re-summarizing, no narrating visible tool calls), the anti-sycophancy line ('agree because it is right, not because the owner said it') and 'say when you do not know'. Its reply rules govern the agent's own replies to the owner: text drafted in someone else's voice (an email, a post, a message written as the owner) keeps that voice, greetings and sign-offs included, and a persona's warmth or curiosity lives inside the answer (review-H670 m-5); its honesty rules hold for everything an agent writes, drafted text included (review-H670b m-1). Its leading maintainer note (stripped before the model sees it, _strip_maintainer_note, agents/core/agent.py:453) says never to add 'be targeted and efficient in your exploration', and why. The repo-root SOUL.md mirrors the text, so agents working on the repo read the contract the product ships; a test keeps the two equal. identity_path (agents/core/agent.py:438) resolves it as soul_path_for does a persona: the data home's souls/IDENTITY.local.md, then a gitignored agents/_identity/IDENTITY.local.md, then the shipped file. read_identity (agents/core/agent.py:481) runs it through the same H387 helpers as a SOUL (the injection scan on the uncapped body, then the cap), announces a flagged or truncated contract (its blocked and quarantined stubs name the contract, not a persona), caps it at its own 4,000 characters (IDENTITY_MAX_CHARS, never above the persona's cap, so the two bands stay within the persona cap plus 4,000), and reads it once per settled file signature for the whole process. An override that cannot be read (not UTF-8, a directory, no permission) is reported at ERROR and the shipped contract used instead, cached against the shipped file's signature so an edit of it is seen; a contract that cannot be located or read at all leaves the agent without it; none of these stops an agent from being built. Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) is the contract, then the agent's persona (either may be empty; an agent without a SOUL still gets the contract). Every place a system prompt is built uses it: Agent.process (agents/core/agent.py:1026) and the synthesis path in agent.py, and the orchestrator's two sites (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2342, agents/core/orchestrator.py:4066) through _system_prompt_of (agents/core/orchestrator.py:485). At a compaction boundary refresh_soul (agents/core/agent.py:730) re-reads the contract as it re-reads the persona (_refresh_identity, agents/core/agent.py:599): a few stats when unchanged; when the file that resolves is absent, vanishes before the read or cannot be read, the contract in force is kept, reported at the boundary as failed-open and warned once per episode (an override absent for an instant resolves to the shipped file for that boundary, as a persona's does); and a moved contract reported as rebuilt, so an edit reaches running agents without a restart. The contract in force at start is committed to the prompt VC (SoulVersionStore) as _identity (_record_identity_version, agents/core/orchestrator.py:467), a no-op when unchanged or empty, so its history is recorded and diffable through /api/admin/prompts/_identity/... beside the personas; what is served is the file (a rollback in the VC does not change it), and an edit picked up at a boundary is recorded at the next start. The prompt-size report counts it as a shared component every call pays (identity_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:136), and the packaged build ships it through its agents/\*/\*.md glob (overrides, \*.local.md, never ship). IDENTITY.md and its .local overlay are in the H506 instruction-file class (INSTRUCTION_BASE_NAMES, agents/core/file_tools.py:259), so a file write to them asks as an instruction file; a test derives the class from every name the contract's and a persona's loaders can resolve. The data home's README lists souls/IDENTITY.local.md. Persona SOULs and Persona.prompt_block (agents/core/cognition/persona.py:142) stay the character layer on top. tests/test_identity_contract.py (18 cases) covers every rule's presence, the banned line's absence and the kept warning, the mirror, the scan verdict, every active agent's system prompt starting with the contract (and exactly contract then persona), an agent without a persona, the orchestrator's helper and all four sites, both overrides and their order, the scan and the cap on an override, an edit reaching a running agent at the boundary, the one-stat unchanged path, a failed re-read keeping the last-good contract, the version record (once, and a failing store never stopping a start) and one read shared across agents; tests/test_h670b_identity_review.py (21 cases) covers the first review: the instruction-file class, a contract absent or vanishing at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 or unreadable override, the contract's own cap, no empty version, the start-time record, the contract's scope, a mid-file comment, the prompt-size component, the ERROR on a flagged contract, the stubs' wording and CHT-117; tests/test_h670c_identity_review.py (14 cases) covers the second review: honesty unscoped, a shipped contract that stops being UTF-8, a vanish before the real read, the hub's start recording the contract, one warning per episode and a second one, a reader that raises, the fallback following a shipped edit, a directory override, a permission error at load and at a boundary, the fallback rules once when both bands are blocked, and the docs. The first review (one major: the contract was outside the instruction-file class; seven minors: a missing contract emptied at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 override stopped every agent, the two bands exceeded the cap, the VC's rollback and A/B claim, the contract's unscoped rules against drafting personas and trait directives, six untested survivors, CHT-117 naming a retired agent; six nits) is answered; its six real survivors are pinned. The feature's 17 mutants were caught but one equivalent; the first review round's 15 were caught, one only against a stand-in (the vanish case, now driven through the real read). The second review (no major; three minors: the scope lifted the honesty rules from drafted text, a shipped contract that stopped being UTF-8 emptied the contract at a boundary, three real survivors; eight nits) is answered; its 31 mutants' three real survivors are pinned, and this round's 10 were all caught, two after their cases were added (a reader that raises, a second warning episode). Known limits: the prompt-size report measures the shipped contract, not an override in force (as it measures shipped personas); PromptRefresh.text is still the persona alone; the contract's scan verdict is reported only in the log (a persona's also reaches GET /api/agents/{id}/soul); the data home's README is written once, so an existing install does not get its new row. Test manual: CHT-117; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md names the band. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Architecture citation review (2026-10-02): only the LLM video paragraph was corrected from declaration-only to current approved ToolRPC dispatch. Shared identity text, loader, prompt composition and cited identity behavior are unchanged; no status promotion. | <a id="h687"></a>H687 | A recurring instruction runs once immediately, then on its cadence | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch and reworked after two reviews. A recurring instruction now runs once when it is created, then on its cadence, with Hermes' split kept: - Hermes' /loop is an interval. LoopManager.set() starts it with next_due_at=now (hermes_cli/loops.py:466), and the reply says the first wakeup fires now. - A Hermes cron job waits for its first computed run. Nerva applies the /loop behaviour to its cron-style jobs where the schedule is an interval. Before this change, a new Nerva job always waited for its first slot, and every confirmation named only the schedule. Every creation surface goes through JobRunner.arm (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1312): - POST /api/jobs (agents/core/routers/jobs.py:140); - the /remind slash command (agents/core/commands.py:277); - \`nerva jobs create\`, which posts to that route and prints the hub's confirmation (agents/cli/nerva.py:1567-1576); - the HUD arm and JobCreateDialog notes (armNote, frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:38, used at frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:157 and frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:163). JobRunner.create is a thin wrapper around it (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1310). first_run_policy (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:543) decides what runs before its first slot. Only an agent-instruction job does (ask, brief or task, or a script or monitor source), and only on an interval cadence. is_interval_cron (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:517) reads what the cron fires on, not how it is written: every hour with one minute or the minutes of a \`\*/N\`, or one minute in the hours of a \`\*/N\`, with no weekday, day or month restriction. So '0 0-23/2' and '0 0,12' count as '0 \*/2' and '0 \*/12', while two times of day ('0 9,21') and '\*/5 \*/2' (only in even hours) are calendar schedules. These wait for their first slot: - a calendar schedule (a 07:00 morning brief armed in the evening, a weekday job armed on Saturday); - a reminder; - a repeat-limited job, whose early run would spend one of its few attempts (critic note 15); - anything during quiet hours, whose output would be held and delivered after the next fresh run's; - anything whose first slot is near (first_run_margin, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:538): nearer than two minutes or than a quarter of the cadence (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:485-486), read from the real cron in the scheduler's timezone (slot_timing, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1347). "every 12 hours" armed 45 minutes before its noon slot waits for it instead of running twice within the hour. first_run_decision (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:580) applies it at creation. A paused or disabled job never fires, and the create call's \`first_run\` (StrictBool, agents/core/routers/jobs.py:34; \`--first-run/--no-first-run\`, agents/cli/nerva.py:261) overrides the policy either way. It is a creation-time choice, never a stored option: validate_options refuses it, so PATCH cannot store it and no rollback trips on it. The first run is a dispatch request drained by _fire_once (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1460) under the shared job gate (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1337; ORIGINS and the column at agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:12 and agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:40). Its origin is 'first_run' when the policy chose it and 'first_run_asked' when the create call asked for it. - The request is durable, so the drain checks a policy first run against first_run_policy again when it comes to run (first_run_due, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1363; agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:214). By then the job may have been edited into a calendar schedule, a reminder or a repeat-limited job, quiet hours may have begun, or a scheduler that was down may have left its first slot near. The request is then cancelled and names the reason. An asked first run is not second-guessed. - The drain runs both with force=False (agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:223), so _fire_once applies the pause check (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1473), the emergency stop (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1479), option and action validation, the repeat reservation (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1498; for scripts, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:123-124) and the quiet-hours hold on delivery (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1719; for scripts, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:433-435). - Only the owner's own ▶ now runs a paused job. When ▶ now folds into a queued first run, it makes that request manual (agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:86). - Pausing cancels a not-yet-claimed first run of either origin (cancel_queued, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:107, called at agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1432). - An edit that changes what runs (action or options) queues the first run again for the edited job, keeping its origin, instead of letting the claim's identity check drop it (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1411; _requeue_first_run, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1415). A rename or a new schedule leaves it queued. Either way a policy first run meets the policy again at the drain. If the queue refuses the new request, the edit is still saved and says why in the log. If the enqueue is refused at creation (the queue is at capacity, or the database is locked), the answer is still 201 with first_run null and 'first run not queued (…)', because the job is armed and a retry would duplicate it. POST /api/jobs returns the receipt as first_run, or null, plus one confirmation built by arm_confirmation (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:604): - 'first run now, then \<schedule\> (\<cron\>)', or 'first run queued, then …' with a note that nothing fires while the scheduler is not running (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:619); - '\<schedule\> (\<cron\>), on its cadence', with the quiet-hours or near-slot reason when that was the cause. /remind, the CLI and the HUD print it. Tests: - tests/test_job_first_run.py, 74 cases (41 before the second review). 28 of them fail on ccdd7161, with its fixture pointed at that commit's slot helper: - an interval ask job is queued and drained into one run before any slot (tests/test_job_first_run.py:109); - a calendar schedule waits (tests/test_job_first_run.py:123); - the emergency stop (tests/test_job_first_run.py:133); - pause cancels the first run, and one that slips past the pause is still skipped (tests/test_job_first_run.py:143, tests/test_job_first_run.py:155), an asked one too (tests/test_job_first_run.py:451); - ▶ now keeps its force (tests/test_job_first_run.py:164); - edits: one to the action runs the edited job (tests/test_job_first_run.py:176), one the policy would not fire drops it (tests/test_job_first_run.py:387), an asked one survives (tests/test_job_first_run.py:439), a rename leaves it alone (tests/test_job_first_run.py:473), and a refused re-queue still saves (tests/test_job_first_run.py:483); - at the drain: quiet hours that began (tests/test_job_first_run.py:402), a slot now near (tests/test_job_first_run.py:416), and an asked first run that runs anyway (tests/test_job_first_run.py:429); - first_run is a creation choice, never stored (tests/test_job_first_run.py:210); - a refused enqueue and a locked database (tests/test_job_first_run.py:229, tests/test_job_first_run.py:499); - quiet hours, the near slot, a long cadence near its slot and no scheduler (tests/test_job_first_run.py:242, tests/test_job_first_run.py:251, tests/test_job_first_run.py:462, tests/test_job_first_run.py:261); - an interval script job submits its first run (tests/test_job_first_run.py:273); - the interval table, the decision table and the slot timing on the real cron (tests/test_job_first_run.py:306, tests/test_job_first_run.py:339, tests/test_job_first_run.py:520); - both CLI flags (tests/test_job_first_run.py:353, tests/test_job_first_run.py:363). - tests/test_nerva_cli.py:1267 and tests/test_slash_commands.py:317. - frontend/src/test/jobs-first-run.test.ts (armNote). - tests/test_jobs_routes.py: a new reminder still sends nothing until /run, and the script job's /run is again a fresh manual request. Every guard added in the second review was mutated out one at a time, and a test failed each time. 656 job, dispatch, command, CLI and schedule tests pass. One-shot run_at jobs (H450) never take a first run, even when one is asked for: first_run_policy and first_run_decision answer "one-shot" (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:563, :589). The H450 row says they must stay out of the first run. A known pre-existing gap outside this row: parse_schedule drops a window or day qualifier after an interval ("every 30 minutes between 9 and 17" becomes '\*/30 \* \* \* \*'), so such a job runs all day and counts as an interval. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h689"></a>H689 | One stable identity for this install, and per-profile isolation of its runtime artifacts | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva has a durable install identity, one hub per data root and isolated profiles, as Hermes does, credentials included. agents/core/install_identity.py keeps one 32-hex id in \<data root\>/install_id (install_id). It is minted once under an in-process threading lock and a cross-process file lock (fcntl.flock, or msvcrt.locking on Windows, on install_id.lock). It is written atomically: mkstemp, fsync, os.replace, then a directory fsync. It is read back before it is cached and returned. A file holding anything but an id, one that cannot be read, or a write that fails answers None, never a fresh value, and the file is left as it is. The consumers use it. agents/core/first_action.py stores it as the activation clock's install_id; mark_installed no longer re-mints over an unreadable or foreign record, and infer_install_at_boot and record_first_action never write over one. agents/core/node_mesh.py issues node grants as node:\<id\>@\<install\> and keeps hub_id on the record. agents/core/channels/pairing.py stamps each deeplink with hub, and redeem_deeplink refuses another install's (other_install). agents/core/satellite_hub.py gives SatellitePairing a validated hub_id, and SatelliteHub refuses a pairing pinned to another install (other_install, read lazily through _own_hub_id). serve.py stops on a bad JARVIS_PROFILE (paths.profile_error). It holds \<data root\>/hub.lock for the life of the process (acquire_hub_lock), and so does the app lifespan, so a bare uvicorn agents.web:app (docker-compose, CI) is held too; so a second hub on the same root stops with HubAlreadyRunning naming the holder's pid, and it prints the data root and install id. agents/core/paths.py gives JARVIS_PROFILE a meaning. A valid name (profile_name: lowercase letters, digits, - or _, up to 32; "default" is the default root) makes data_root a sibling root, \<data root\>-profiles/\<name\>, which nothing walking the default root reaches; a refused name makes data_root() raise, so every entry fails closed. Settings, memory, the install id and lock, the SecretStore and tokens/ follow it (agents/core/secrets.py resolves DEFAULT_STORE under the root the process starts with). So do its credentials (H689b): a profile's hub reads one file, \<profile root\>/.env (profile_env_file, agents/core/env_provenance.py:440), and neither the repo's nor the data home's .env, which hold the default hub's provider keys, channel bot tokens and admin token (load_hub_env, agents/core/env_provenance.py:454); the process environment still wins, as for any hub. A refused profile name stops the load instead of falling back to the shared files. The load reports the shared files as not read, hub_value (the read that never loads) follows the same rule, the doctor's offline prediction derives the profile's table from its own .env (scripts/doctor.py:746), serve.py names the profile's config file at start, and paths.data_root answers for a given environment so the doctor finds the same root the hub does. The id, its lock and hub.lock are on the forget keep list (data_purge.KEEP_FILES), a clock a forget emptied starts again, and a pre-H689 clock adopts the id. It is classified in env_provenance READ_BEFORE_LOAD and FROM_PROCESS_ONLY. Tests: tests/test_h689_install_identity.py (58) and tests/test_h689b_profile_env.py (13; 14 mutants, all killed, four after their cases were added), including racing processes that agree on one id and a real second-process lock refusal; tests/test_first_run_first_action.py pins the no-re-mint rule; 32 mutants, 30 killed, 2 fixed.
